//! Seeks and row-local scan predicates.
use super::*;

impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.011.000.001#4 — retain the binder's actual choice for why, never infer a new lookup or rewrite.
    pub(super) fn record_selection(&mut self, site: &str, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, result: &str, reason: &str) {
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        let bindings = index.columns.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(position, column)| {
            let key = if named { read.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)) } else { read.keys.get(position) }?;
            Some(format!("{column} = {}", self.catalog.source.operand(&key.value)))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let origin = if self.catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())) { "synthesized; " } else { "" };
        let source = if origin.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; read: {}", self.catalog.read_origins.get(&read.site.to_string()).cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| print::read_expr(read))) };
        let mut selection = format!("selected {} ({bindings}); result: {result}; {origin}{reason}{source}", index.name);
        if !self.variants.is_empty() {
            let when = self.variants.iter().map(|(name, present)| format!("{} {}", name.trim_start_matches("$intentPresent:").trim_start_matches("$intentSearch:"), if *present { "present" } else { "absent" })).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
            selection = format!("when {when}: {selection}");
        }
        self.selections.entry(site.into()).and_modify(|prior| { if !prior.contains(&selection) { prior.push_str("; "); prior.push_str(&selection); } }).or_insert(selection);
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#10 — a covered total unique key reads one row; additional named equalities check that row.
    pub(super) fn read_value(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, mode: ReadMode, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<Expr> {
        let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
        for key in &read.keys { if let Some(field) = &key.column { super::super::row_views::predicate_field(info, field, &read.site)?; } }
        if !read.seeks.is_empty() { return Err(type_error(&read.site, "a seek needs a bounded page", "t[prefix, col >= start] first 50 by index")); }
        if read.keys.is_empty() { return Err(unknown(&read.site, format!("{}[] reads nothing; a point read needs a key", read.table), format!("{}[id]", read.table))); }
        let mut point = read.clone();
        let implicit_group = mode == ReadMode::FilteredPoint && info.maintained.is_some() && point.index.is_none();
        if implicit_group { point.index = Some("byGroup".into()); }
        let (mut keys, index, mut selection) = self.select_index(info, &point, mode, out)?;
        super::super::point_key::check(info, &index, read, &keys.iter().map(|(column, _, ty)|
            (column.as_deref(), !ty.is_optional() && *ty != Ty::Unknown)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), false)?;
        if implicit_group { selection = "complete maintained group key".into(); }
        let extra = keys.len() > index.columns.len();
        let mut equalities = Vec::new();
        if extra {
            for (column, value, ty) in &mut keys {
                let column = column.as_ref().expect("named key");
                if column != "id" && !info.columns.contains_key(column) {
                    return Err(unknown(&read.site, format!("{} has no column {column}", read.table), name_rewrite(column, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str).chain(["id"]), None)));
                }
                let expected = self.key_ty(info, &index, column);
                if !ty.comparable(&expected) { return Err(type_error(&read.site, format!("{column} is {}; {}.{column} is {}", ty.name(), read.table, expected.name()), format!("use {} for {column}", expected.name()))); }
                *value = self.snapshot(value.clone(), out);
                if !index.columns.contains(column) { equalities.push((column.clone(), value.clone())); }
            }
            keys.retain(|(column, _, _)| index.columns.contains(column.as_ref().unwrap()));
        }
        let key = self.order_keys(info, &index, keys)?;
        let location = self.site();
        self.record_selection(&location, read, &index, if mode == ReadMode::Exists { "exists" } else if info.maintained.is_some() { "scalar" } else { "row or null" }, &selection);
        self.sources.insert(location.clone(), (read.site.clone(), print::read_expr(read)));
        let mut point = Read { site: location, table: read.table.clone(), index: index.name, key, provenance: KeyProvenance::Shape };
        // @ref LLP 3000.028#3.4 — only a caller-proved existence read may use the whole owner partition.
        if mode == ReadMode::Exists && equalities.is_empty() {
            super::super::own_keys::existence(self.schema, &mut point);
        }
        if equalities.is_empty() { return Ok(if mode == ReadMode::Exists { Expr::Exists(point) } else { Expr::Get(point) }); }
        let row = self.temp("probe");
        out.push(Stmt::Let { name: row.clone(), value: Expr::Get(point) });
        let mut condition = Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Ne, left: Box::new(ir::var(&row)), right: Box::new(Expr::Literal(Value::Null)) };
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a denied aggregate carries a null value, not a present group.
        if mode == ReadMode::Exists && info.maintained.is_some() {
            let present = Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Ne, left: Box::new(ir::field(ir::var(&row), "value")), right: Box::new(Expr::Literal(Value::Null)) };
            condition = Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::And, left: Box::new(condition), right: Box::new(present) };
        }
        for (column, value) in equalities {
            let test = Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left: Box::new(ir::field(ir::var(&row), &column)), right: Box::new(value) };
            condition = Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::And, left: Box::new(condition), right: Box::new(test) };
        }
        if mode == ReadMode::Exists { return Ok(condition); }
        out.push(Stmt::If { condition, then_body: Vec::new(), else_body: vec![Stmt::Assign { name: row.clone(), value: Expr::Literal(Value::Null) }] });
        Ok(ir::var(&row))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.3 — a seek constrains the first index column after the pinned prefix.
    pub(super) fn seek(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<Option<ir::Range>> {
        if read.seeks.is_empty() { return Ok(None); }
        let mut range = ir::Range { gt: None, gte: None, lt: None, lte: None };
        for bound in &read.seeks {
            if index.columns.get(read.keys.len()) != Some(&bound.column) || read.matching.is_some() {
                return Err(type_error(&bound.site, "a seek compares the first unpinned column of a plain index", "t[prefix, col >= start, col < end] first 50 by index"));
            }
            let expected = self.key_ty(info, index, &bound.column);
            let (value, ty) = self.field_value(&ObjectField { spread: false, name: bound.column.clone(), value: Some(bound.value.clone()), site: bound.site.clone() }, &expected, out)?;
            if !ty.comparable(&expected) || ty.is_optional() {
                let endpoint = print::expression(&bound.value);
                let rewrite = if ty.is_optional() {
                    format!("put the read inside `if {endpoint} != null:`, then `{} where .{} {} {endpoint}`", read.table, bound.column, bound.op)
                } else {
                    format!("use a present {} endpoint in `{} where .{} {} {endpoint}`", expected.required().name(), read.table, bound.column, bound.op)
                };
                return Err(type_error(&bound.site, format!("seek on {}.{} expects present {}, got {}", read.table, bound.column, expected.required().name(), ty.name()), rewrite));
            }
            let slot = match bound.op.as_str() { ">" => &mut range.gt, ">=" => &mut range.gte, "<" => &mut range.lt, "<=" => &mut range.lte, _ => unreachable!("parser seek comparison") };
            if slot.is_some() { return Err(type_error(&bound.site, "a seek bound is repeated", "write one lower and one upper bound")); }
            *slot = Some(Box::new(self.snapshot(value, out)));
        }
        if (range.gt.is_some() && range.gte.is_some()) || (range.lt.is_some() && range.lte.is_some()) {
            return Err(type_error(&read.site, "a seek has at most one lower and one upper bound", "t[prefix, col >= start, col < end] first 50 by index"));
        }
        // Null sorts below every present value. An upper-only nullable seek
        // must skip the entire null segment, including its id suffixes.
        if range.gt.is_none() && range.gte.is_none()
            && read.seeks.first().is_some_and(|bound| self.key_ty(info, index, &bound.column).is_optional()) {
            range.gt = Some(Box::new(ir::lit(Value::Null)));
        }
        Ok(Some(range))
    }
}

impl Binder<'_> {
    pub(super) fn variant(&self) -> Option<ir::VariantId> {
        (!self.variants.is_empty()).then(|| {
            let hash = snapback4_core::value::digest(&self.variants);
            std::array::from_fn(|i| u32::from_str_radix(&hash[i * 8..i * 8 + 8], 16).expect("hex digest"))
        })
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.5 — a declared page shape after a scalar predicate needs no wrapper or correction loop.
    pub(super) fn page_shape(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<A> {
        let predicate = read.filter.as_ref()?;
        let (base, name, site) = trailing_shape(predicate)?;
        self.catalog.shapes.get(name)?;
        // Type the operand in isolation: no speculative setup, notes or source sites
        // escape. A valid row/object shape inside the predicate keeps its meaning.
        let mut probe = self.clone();
        probe.focal = Some(("$filter".into(), Ty::Row(read.table.clone())));
        probe.kind = ProgramKind::Query;
        probe.in_predicate = true;
        let (_, ty) = probe.expr(base, &mut setup::Body::default()).ok()?;
        if matches!(ty.required(), Ty::Row(_) | Ty::Object(_) | Ty::RowShape(_) | Ty::RowObject(_) | Ty::Page(_, _) | Ty::Collection(_, _) | Ty::List(_, _)) { return None; }
        let mut page = read.clone();
        remove_trailing_shape(page.filter.as_mut()?);
        Some(A::ShapeUse { base: Box::new(A::Read(page)), name: name.into(), site: site.clone() })
    }

    pub(super) fn scan_filter(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl) -> Fallible<(ReadExpr, Option<Box<Expr>>)> {
        let mut read = read.clone();
        let Some(mut predicate) = read.filter.take() else { return Ok((read, None)); };
        // Planning knows parameter declarations; binding also knows helper,
        // local and lane selectors. Only present, infallible endpoints may
        // leave the short-circuit predicate in either pass.
        let required = self.scopes.iter().flat_map(|scope| scope.keys())
            .filter(|name| self.binding(name).is_some_and(|binding| !binding.ty.is_optional()))
            .cloned().collect();
        if !read.order_keys.is_empty() && read.matching.is_none() {
            let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
            let mut parts = Vec::new(); super::super::intent::split(*predicate, &mut parts);
            let mut residual = Vec::new();
            for part in parts {
                let candidate = super::super::intent::comparison(&part).map(|(column, op, value)| (column, vec![op], value))
                    .or_else(|| super::super::intent::equality(&part, info)
                        .filter(|(column, _)| !self.key_ty(info, index, column).is_optional())
                        .map(|(column, value)| (column, vec![">=".into(), "<=".into()], value)));
                if let Some((column, ops, value)) = candidate
                    && index.columns.get(read.keys.len()) == Some(&column)
                    && super::super::intent_aggregates::safe_endpoint(&value, &required)
                    && ops.iter().all(|op| !read.seeks.iter().any(|bound| bound.op.starts_with('<') == op.starts_with('<'))) {
                    for op in ops { read.seeks.push(ast::SeekBound { column: column.clone(), op, value: value.clone(), site: part.site().clone() }); }
                } else { residual.push(part); }
            }
            let Some(remaining) = super::super::intent::conjunction(residual) else { return Ok((read, None)); };
            predicate = remaining;
        }
        // A single next-column comparison is the same seek as its key spelling.
        if read.seeks.is_empty() && read.matching.is_none()
            && let A::Binary { op, left, right, site } = predicate.as_ref()
            && matches!(op.as_str(), ">" | ">=" | "<" | "<=")
            && let A::Member { base, name, .. } = left.as_ref()
            && matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Focal { .. })
            && index.columns.get(read.keys.len()) == Some(name)
            && !has_focal(right)
            && super::super::intent_aggregates::safe_endpoint(right, &required)
        {
            read.seeks.push(ast::SeekBound { column: name.clone(), op: op.clone(), value: *right.clone(), site: site.clone() });
            self.notes.push(Diagnostic::new(site, "N_SEEK", "a `where` on the next index column is a seek; write it in the key", print::read_expr(&read)).guide("reads"));
            return Ok((read, None));
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.3 — an unscoped filter can spend the whole page meter on unrelated authors.
        if self.kind == ProgramKind::Query && read.keys.is_empty() && read.seeks.is_empty() && read.matching.is_none()
            && crate::bind::unique_bound::group_bound(self.schema, &read.table, &[]).is_none_or(|n| n > 128)
            && has_focal(&predicate) && !pins_leading_column(&predicate, index.columns.first().map(String::as_str))
        {
            let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
            let follows = follow_edge_filter(&predicate, info);
            let consequence = if follows {
                "pages come back short and incomplete when followed authors are sparse (`complete: false`)"
            } else { "sparse matches can exhaust the row meter and return short `complete: false` pages" };
            // @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — wave 2 (024): a `where` every row passes is the trivial repair.
            let rewrite = match always_true(&predicate, info) {
                Some(column) => format!("drop this `where`: .{column} is required, so every row passes it"),
                None if follows => "pin a column with `where .col = value`, choose `by .col`, or read a `view` (see `snapback4 guide feeds`)".to_owned(),
                None => "pin a column with `where .col = value`, choose `by .col`, read a `view` for a count or total, or drop a `where` every row passes".to_owned(),
            };
            self.notes.push(Diagnostic::new(predicate.site(), "N_SPARSE_FILTER",
                format!("{} by {}: this unscoped `where` pins no index prefix; {consequence}", read.table, index.name),
                rewrite).guide(if follows { "feeds" } else { "reads" }));
        }
        let value = self.row_predicate(&read.table, &predicate)?;
        Ok((read, Some(Box::new(value))))
    }

    pub(super) fn row_predicate(&mut self, table: &str, predicate: &A) -> Fallible<Expr> {
        let row_ty = Ty::Row(table.into());
        let prior = self.focal.replace(("$filter".into(), row_ty.clone()));
        let kind = std::mem::replace(&mut self.kind, ProgramKind::Query);
        let predicate_context = std::mem::replace(&mut self.in_predicate, true);
        let mut body = setup::Body::default();
        self.push_scope();
        // A fresh lexical row snapshot lets short-circuit guards narrow its
        // fields without leaking facts to another scan or an enclosing row.
        self.scopes.last_mut().unwrap().insert("$filter".into(), Binding {
            runtime: "$filter".into(), declared: row_ty.clone(), ty: row_ty,
            parameter: false, revision: 0, fields: BTreeMap::new(), error: None, origins: Vec::new(),
        });
        let bound = self.expr(predicate, &mut body);
        self.pop_scope();
        self.kind = kind;
        self.in_predicate = predicate_context;
        self.focal = prior;
        let (value, ty) = bound?;
        if !ty.is_bool() { return Err(type_error(predicate.site(), format!("where needs a bool predicate, got {}", ty.name()), "where .col = value")); }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.6 — predicate setup, including lazy reads, runs afresh for each rule-visible row.
        Ok(if body.is_empty() { value } else { Expr::Block { body: body.code, value: Box::new(value) } })
    }
}

fn pins_leading_column(expr: &A, column: Option<&str>) -> bool {
    let focal_column = |value: &A| matches!(value, A::Member { base, name, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Focal { .. }) && Some(name.as_str()) == column);
    match expr {
        A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" => pins_leading_column(left, column) || pins_leading_column(right, column),
        A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => pins_leading_column(left, column) && pins_leading_column(right, column),
        A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "=" => (focal_column(left) && !has_focal(right)) || (focal_column(right) && !has_focal(left)),
        _ => false,
    }
}

fn has_focal(expr: &A) -> bool {
    match expr {
        A::Focal { .. } => true,
        A::Name { .. } | A::Literal { .. } => false,
        A::Member { base, .. } | A::Unary { value: base, .. } => has_focal(base),
        A::Binary { left, right, .. } => has_focal(left) || has_focal(right),
        A::Call { args, .. } => args.iter().any(has_focal),
        // Other forms stay predicates; a read on a row-dependent key must not escape the row.
        _ => true,
    }
}

use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState, engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
fn id(s:&str)->Value{Value::Id(s.into())}
fn text(s:&str)->Value{Value::String(s.into())}
fn args(v:&[(&str,Value)])->BTreeMap<String,Value>{v.iter().map(|(k,v)|(k.to_string(),v.clone())).collect()}
fn engine(source:&str)->Engine{let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap()}
fn why(e:&mut Engine,op:&str,who:&str,values:&[(&str,Value)])->snapback4_core::Refusal{match e.mutate(op,None,Context::new(who,args(values)).at(100)).unwrap().state{WriteState::Failed {why,..}=>why,s=>panic!("{s:?}")}}
const UNIQUE:&str="table rows:\n  owner: principal\n  writer: principal\n  handle: text <=20\n  other: text <=20 ?\n  unique handle\n  unique other else OTHER_TAKEN 'Other key is taken'\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .writer = viewer\n  update <- .writer = viewer\nmutation add(id: id, owner: principal, writer: principal, handle: text <=20, other: text <=20 ?):\n  return insert rows {id,owner,writer,handle,other}\nmutation absent(id: id, owner: principal, writer: principal, handle: text <=20, other: text <=20 ?):\n  return insert rows {id,owner,writer,handle,other} if absent\nquery page(c: cursor ?):\n  return rows first 10 by .id after c\n";
fn seed(e:&mut Engine,id_:&str,owner:&str,handle:&str,other:Option<&str>){e.seed(vec![("rows".into(),snapback4_core::value::object([("id",id(id_)),("owner",id(owner)),("writer",id(owner)),("handle",text(handle)),("other",other.map(text).unwrap_or(Value::Null))]))]).unwrap();}
fn input(id_:&str,owner:&str,handle:&str,other:Option<&str>)->Vec<(&'static str,Value)>{vec![("id",id(id_)),("owner",id(owner)),("writer",id(owner)),("handle",text(handle)),("other",other.map(text).unwrap_or(Value::Null))]}
#[test]
fn visible_unique_names_the_declaration_and_authored_code_wins(){
 let mut e=engine(UNIQUE);seed(&mut e,"old","alice","key",Some("other"));
 let visible=why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("new","alice","key",None));
 assert_eq!(visible.code,"E_UNIQUE");assert!(visible.message.contains("unique handle") && !visible.message.contains("$intent"));
 let named=why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("new","alice","different",Some("other")));assert_eq!(named.code,"OTHER_TAKEN");
 assert_eq!(why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("old","alice","different",None)).code,"E_CONSTRAINT");
 let absent=e.mutate("absent",None,Context::new("alice",args(&input("new","alice","key",None))).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(absent.state,WriteState::Sent {result:Value::Null,..}));
 // Null secondary keys do not conflict; the handle remains independently unique.
 for (i,handle) in [("two","two"),("three","three")] {assert!(matches!(e.mutate("add",None,Context::new("alice",args(&input(i,"alice",handle,None))).at(100)).unwrap().state,WriteState::Sent {..}));}
}
#[test]
fn hidden_unique_is_the_same_public_refusal_as_rule_denial_including_seats(){
 for viewer in ["alice","service:ops"] {
  let mut e=engine(UNIQUE);seed(&mut e,"old","bob","key",None);
  let collision=why(&mut e,"add",viewer,&input("new",viewer,"key",None));
  let mut denied=input("new",viewer,"different",None);denied[2].1=id("other");
  let denial=why(&mut e,"add",viewer,&denied);
  assert_eq!(collision,denial,"{viewer}");assert_eq!(collision.code,"E_RULE");assert!(collision.message.contains("a row you cannot read holds this key"));
  if viewer.starts_with("service:"){assert!(collision.message.contains("rows rule clause: insert <- .writer = viewer"));}
  assert_eq!(e.store().rows("rows").unwrap().len(),1);
 }
}
#[test]
fn hidden_counter_is_request_local_and_changes_no_query_envelope_or_meter(){
 let mut e=engine(UNIQUE);seed(&mut e,"a","alice","a",None);seed(&mut e,"b","bob","b",None);
 let plain=e.query("page",Context::new("alice",args(&[("c",Value::Null)])).at(100)).unwrap();
 let trace=RefusalTrace::default();
 let traced=e.query("page",Context::new("alice",args(&[("c",Value::Null)])).at(100).with_refusal_trace(trace.clone())).unwrap();
 assert_eq!(serde_json::to_value(plain).unwrap(),serde_json::to_value(traced).unwrap());
 assert_eq!(trace.hidden(),1);
 let next=RefusalTrace::default();e.query("page",Context::new(snapback4_core::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL,args(&[("c",Value::Null)])).at(100).with_refusal_trace(next.clone())).unwrap();assert_eq!(next.hidden(),0);
}
#[test]
fn ephemeral_seat_denials_have_the_same_static_clause(){
 let mut e=engine("table typing:\n  owner: principal\n  unique owner\n  cap 50\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  ephemeral 4s\nmutation add(owner: principal):\n  return insert typing {owner}\n");
 let denied=why(&mut e,"add","service:ops",&[("owner",id("alice"))]);assert_eq!(denied.code,"E_RULE");assert!(denied.message.ends_with("typing rule clause: insert <- .owner = viewer"),"{denied:?}");
}

}

  never by a mapping reconstructed from column names or types; principal
  partitions are tagged so no principal's name collides with `public`; a
  commit is delivered whole however large; a grant that ends inside its
  own commit delivers nothing; a delivered aggregate's audience is its
  source's. Horizons bound acquisition, not holdings: a device holds what
  was delivered, and a local read past the horizon says `complete: false`.
- **2026-09-12 — completeness is a class, not a count (lead, after the
  meter review).** As Snapback 2 (LLP 1001 §3): a query with a scan the
  prefix does not decide is `complete: false` whatever the meter found,
  and a capped page's `next` continues from its last visible row or ends
  on an empty page; the meter's numbers, its cut site and a refusal's
  count stay off the viewer wire; a hidden row costs what a miss costs and
  a hoisted-false range what an empty one costs. The device runs the same
  hoist; its `complete` speaks of the horizon and the meter alone, since a

  **2026-09-15 — W7c development inspection (author's lane brief).**
  Loopback `dev` may return the existing engine counters on successful reads
  explicitly requesting `x-snapback-meter: 1`; the online client exposes this
  as `createClient({ meter: true })` and optional `Read.meter`. The counters
  describe the returned server answer, including a remembered watch answer.
  explicit compatibility store, and refreshes a near-expiry session before it
  becomes unusable.

- **2026-09-13 — hidden rows stay hidden at write and replay boundaries
  (security review).** A unique conflict with a row the caller cannot read is
  the generic rule refusal. Audience insertion is admitted only when the
  caller already has the matching grant; audience keys cannot be changed to
  acquire one. The private mutation log may retain old operator data, but sync
  catch-up re-evaluates the current row and emits only its current readable
  image, or a deletion for a row that disappeared or expired. Previously
  delivered device data remains a property of that device.

- **2026-10-12 — a third asset kind, `file`, under the same contract
  (author, approving LLP 3000.038).** A document column (`file <=N`, with an
  optional allow-list of media types Snapback can verify) keeps one home,
  serving by the home row's read rule and the release/sweep lifetime above.
  The upload declares its type and filename (`?as=file&name=`); the server
  checks the declaration against the bytes and never stores a script-bearing
  type. A file is only ever served as an attachment (`nosniff`, a
  sandboxing CSP); `once` does not apply; nothing is transcoded.

- **2026-09-13 — assets are staged, bounded and viewer-private (security
  review).** Server IDs use operating-system or Web Crypto entropy. Upload
  bytes and asset deletion run outside the engine mutex, with bounded slots
  and a generation/session check before commit. A once asset is consumable by
  its placed recipient, while its unplaced uploader retains preview access;
  ranged reads cannot bypass consumption. Image dimensions, total pixels and
  video duration are capped. Unsatisfiable ranges return 416 and every asset
  response is `private, no-store`, so a browser cache cannot cross principals.

This is a correction the received review made to rev 1's claim that the
interpreter "needs almost nothing"; it needs this.

### 5.1 The seven forms (rev 1)

| # | Today | Proposed | Source | Bound by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | A local binds once ("duplicate local") | `x = expr` rebinds a local of the same type; a `for` may carry a counter or a running sum | S | the step meter; `Stmt::Assign` exists |
| 2 | No accumulators | `xs = []` then `xs = xs + [v]` (the binder emits `Push`); `s = s + n`; a list built in a loop is bounded by the heap meter and may be iterated | S | the heap meter (`Push` charges) and the step meter |
| 3 | `continue` refused | `continue` | S (minimalism) | `Stmt::Continue` exists |
| 4 | `else if` refused | `else if cond:` as sugar for the nesting (`elif` is a guess `check --fix` rewrites, §5.5) | S (minimalism) | nothing to bound |
| 5 | `where` refused | `t[prefix] [after c] first\|last N by idx where <predicate>`: the scan proceeds in index order; a row passing the read rule and the predicate counts toward N; examined rows and probes are metered | S + A's completeness class | the examined-rows and probe meters; §5.6 fixes completeness and cursors |
| 6 | Text `+` refused | `text + text` → `Concat`; `slice`, `tokens`, `count(text)`, `lower(text)`; no `format`, no interpolation | S (bytes) and a product discipline | the output and heap meters |
| 7 | `/` and `%` refused | `a / b` (integer quotient) and `a % b` beside `divmod`; zero refuses `E_DIV_ZERO` | S (a semantic preference) | nothing to bound |

**2026-09-27 — list expressions (0.4.5 lane QE).** `a + b` concatenates
two lists in every expression position, including returns, arguments and
record fields. Item types join as in list literals: incompatible nonempty
items refuse immediately, including references to different tables and
conflicts nested inside records or lists. Empty lists supply no competing
item type. Compatible scalar items retain a common representation. Two enums
join to their deduplicated variant union, including subsets and equivalent sets
in different orders; an existing superset keeps its order, otherwise the union
follows encounter order. Quarry imposes no variant-count limit. Self-append
checks both operands against the joined item type, so disjoint enums also
keep their original string values. Enum and text widen to text bounded by both
the longest variant and the text operand,
under the existing text character-domain rule. If an enum item cannot join,
the diagnostic teaches a typed identity helper such as
`fn state(v: enum('on', 'off')): v`, with all variants, and
`[state(a), state(b)]` when every value belongs to that enum; this preserves
values without conversion. Generic id with a reference or
principal becomes id; int with decimal or money retains that numeric type
without rescaling; json accepts other known, non-row values. Unknown is never
a widening candidate in a nonempty item join. A mixed-reference refusal teaches
the intentional loss of table specificity: `fn exposedId(value: id): value`,
then `[exposedId(x), exposedId(y)]`, or `exposedId(row.id)` in each projected
field for a nested `[].id` conflict. An effect's `once [recipient, period]` is a positional key tuple,
not an iterable list, so its coordinates may differ in type. Bounds add, saturating
at the existing meter-bound sentinel. The shared `Concat` builtin charges
the result allocation. `xs = xs + [x]` retains its in-place `Push` lowering.
Other loop-carried concatenations retain the existing heap-bound widening
when their item type stabilizes; recursive item growth refuses with a
self-append rewrite using a fixed item type. A list plus a non-list names
both operand types and suggests `xs + [x]`. The recursive-growth rewrite
also covers concatenations within conditional assignments and failures caught
at reassignment, after the assigned expression typechecks; an ill-typed `+`
keeps its operand diagnostic without a recursive-growth cascade. Consuming
the accumulator in a scalar, such as `xs = xs + [count(xs)]`, also keeps the
ordinary item-join diagnostic; only embedding the accumulator in the resulting
container earns recursive-growth guidance.
The length census prices bounded append and prepend increments
like `Push`, including the initial contents; doubling and unresolved cyclic or
open growth retain the step-ceiling fallback. No runtime ceiling changes.

### 5.2 Composition: helpers with bodies, jobs with preludes

**8. `fn` takes a body.** Today `fn` is one expression and `proc` is a
block that returns nothing, so the 0.1.0 builder wrote the receipt
fan-out three times (`send`, `sendMedia`, `sendPoll`) and the 0.2.6
builder wrote its receipt state as three nested one-line `fn`s. LLP
2000.000 §6 named the split as pricing residue. Proposed: one `fn`,
declared with an expression or with a block ending in `return`, callable
in expression position anywhere; expanded inline as helpers are today
(`bind/program/control.rs`), so a write inside a body is admitted exactly
when the caller may write. **Loop placement also inherits the caller's
enclosing loops:** a helper inside a job's recipient sweep performs ordinary
per-recipient loops; a helper at the job's top level still receives the
automatic-sweep and stable-domain checks. `break` and `continue` remain
lexically scoped to loops inside the helper. A query calling a writing `fn`
is a placement error at the call site, with the site named. `proc` is deleted with the
rewrite `fn` (rules: delete, don't deprecate).

```quarry
fn receiptState(cid: conversations, sender: principal, seq: int):
  unread = 0
  undelivered = 0
  for m in memberships[cid] first 50 by byConversationPrincipal:
    if m.principal = sender or m.joinedSequence >= seq:
      continue
    if m.readSequence < seq:
      unread = unread + 1
    if m.deliveredSequence < seq:
      undelivered = undelivered + 1
  return 'read' if unread = 0 else ('delivered' if undelivered = 0 else 'sent')
```

**2026-09-27 — repeated pure calls (0.4.5 lane QE, calibration #22).**
A null test narrows a repeated `fn` call like a field path of a lexical
snapshot when the expanded helper reads no table, writes nothing and reads
neither `now` nor another changing input (the commit/read clock, a minted id
or connection state). Every argument must be a lexical name, a field path
of a lexical snapshot, a literal, or another such pure call. For example,
`now + windows.ttl(c.disappearing) if windows.ttl(c.disappearing) != null else null`
compiles. A database read used directly as an argument does not qualify;
binding its result once creates a lexical snapshot.

//! Column and parameter types, including precise unsupported-default repairs.
use super::Parser;
use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Fallible, Site, syntax, syntax_with}, lexer::Kind, print};
impl Parser {
    pub(super) fn parse_args(&mut self) -> Fallible<Vec<ArgDecl>> {
        self.take("(")?;
        let mut args = Vec::new();
        while !self.at(")") {
            let name = self.peek().clone();
            let binding = self.local_name()?;
            self.take(":")?;
            let type_site = self.peek().site.clone();
            let type_ = self.parse_type(false).map_err(|mut d| {
                if d.mechanical && d.message.starts_with("once is a column modifier") {
                    // Nested element types need a whole-list rewrite, not an argument fragment.
                    if d.site == type_site { d.site = name.site.clone(); d.rewrite = format!("{binding}: {}", d.rewrite); }
                    else { d.mechanical = false; }
                }
                d
            })?;
            if self.at("=") || self.at("default") {
                let at = self.peek().site.clone();
                self.position += 1;
                let fallback = self.expression(0, &[",", ")"])?;
                let mut optional = type_.clone(); optional.optional = true;
                return Err(syntax_with(&at, "operation parameters have no literal defaults; omission is null for an optional parameter",
                    format!("declare `{binding}: {}` and bind `{binding}Value = {binding} ?? {}` at the start of the body", print::scalar_type(&optional), print::expression(&fallback))).guide("types"));
            }
            args.push(ArgDecl { name: binding, type_, site: name.site });
            if !self.maybe(",") {
                break;
            }
        }
        self.take(")")?;
        Ok(args)
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_type(&mut self, column: bool) -> Fallible<ScalarType> {
        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
        let mut kind;
        if self.maybe("[") {
            let item = self.parse_type(column)?;
            self.take("]")?;
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — list ranges share text's inclusive spelling.
            let bounds_error = |site: &Site| syntax_with(site, "list bounds require <=N or an inclusive a..b range", "write [T] <=N or [T] a..b, for example [int] 1..8").guide("types");
            let number = |parser: &mut Self| -> Fallible<u32> {
                if parser.at("-") {
                    return Err(syntax(&parser.peek().site, "list bounds must be nonnegative integers"));
                }
                if !parser.at_kind(Kind::Number) { return Err(bounds_error(&parser.peek().site)); }
                let token = parser.take_kind(Kind::Number)?;
                if token.value.contains('.') {
                    return Err(syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers; fractional bounds are not allowed"));
                }
                token.value.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers from 0 through 4294967295"))
            };
            let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
                (0, number(self)?)
            } else {
                let min = number(self)?;
                if !self.maybe(".") || !self.maybe(".") { return Err(bounds_error(&self.peek().site)); }
                (min, number(self)?)
            };
            if min > max {
                return Err(syntax(&site, "list minimum must not exceed its maximum"));
            }
            if item.optional {
                return Err(syntax(&site, "list element types cannot be optional"));
            }
            kind = TypeKind::List { item: Box::new(item), min, max };
        } else {
            let name = self.word()?;
            kind = match name.value.as_str() {
                "text" => {
                    let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
                        (None, self.number()?)
                    } else if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) {
                        let min = self.number()?;
                        self.take(".")?;
                        self.take(".")?;
                        (Some(min), self.number()?)
                    } else {
                        // The default is normalized here; every downstream
                        // validator and resource calculation sees the same bound.
                        (None, 10_000)
                    };
                    let mut format = None;
                    if self.maybe("format") {
                        let value = self.word()?;
                        if value.value != "handle" && value.value != "url" {
                            let bound = match min {
                                Some(min) => format!("{min}..{max}"),
                                None => format!("<={max}"),
                            };
                            return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("unknown text format {}", value.value), format!("use text {bound} format handle or text {bound} format url")));
                        }
                        format = Some(value.value);
                    }
                    TypeKind::Text { min, max, format }
                }
                "decimal" => TypeKind::Decimal { scale: self.scale("decimal 12")? },
                "money" => {
                    let currency = self.word()?;
                    if currency.value.len() != 3 || !currency.value.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_uppercase()) {
                        return Err(syntax_with(&currency.site, "money currency must be exactly three uppercase letters", "use money USD or money USD 2"));
                    }
                    let scale = if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) { self.scale(&format!("money {} 12", currency.value))? } else { 2 };
                    TypeKind::Money { currency: currency.value, scale }
                }
                "json" => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    TypeKind::Json { max: self.number()? }
                }
                "bytes" => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    TypeKind::Bytes { max: self.number()? }
                }
                "image" => {
                    if !self.maybe("<=") { return Err(syntax_with(&site, "image needs a maximum upload size in bytes", "write `image <=5MB` (5,000,000 bytes), optionally followed by once or ?").guide("types")); }
                    TypeKind::Image { max: self.media_size()?, once: false }
                }
                "video" => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    TypeKind::Video { max: self.media_size()? }
                }
                // `file` is a type only before `<=`: a table named `file` stays a reference.
                // @ref LLP 3000.038#1 — the allow-list names only types the server can verify against the bytes.
                "file" if self.at("<=") => {
                    self.take("<=")?;
                    let max = self.number()?;
                    let mut types = Vec::new();
                    if self.maybe("(") {
                        while !self.at(")") {
                            let value = self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?;
                            if let Some(why) = snapback4_core::assets::file_type_refusal(&value.value) {
                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, why, format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf', 'text/plain')")).guide("assets"));
                            }
                            if types.contains(&value.value) {
                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("'{}' is listed twice", value.value), "list each media type once"));
                            }
                            types.push(value.value);
                            if !self.maybe(",") { break; }
                        }
                        self.take(")")?;
                        if types.is_empty() {
                            return Err(syntax_with(&site, "a file allow-list needs at least one media type", format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf'), or file <={max} for every verifiable type")).guide("assets"));
                        }
                    }
                    TypeKind::File { max, types }
                }
                "cursor" => TypeKind::Cursor,
                "id" => TypeKind::Id,
                "principal" => TypeKind::Principal,
                "int" if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) || matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "<=" | ">=" | "<" | ">" | "-" | ".") => {
                    let (min, max) = self.int_range(&name.site)?;
                    TypeKind::IntRange { min, max }
                }
                "int" => TypeKind::Int,
                "time" => TypeKind::Time,
                "bool" => TypeKind::Bool,
                "enum" => {
                    self.take("(")?;
                    let mut variants = Vec::new();
                    while !self.at(")") {
                        variants.push(self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?.value);
                        if !self.maybe(",") {
                            break;
                        }
                    }
                    self.take(")")?;
                    if variants.is_empty() {
                        return Err(syntax_with(&site, "enum needs at least one variant", "enum('draft', 'published')"));
                    }
                    TypeKind::Enum { variants }
                }
                other => TypeKind::Ref { table: other.to_owned() },
            };
        }
        if self.maybe("once") {
            if !column {
                let optional = self.maybe("?");
                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is a column modifier, not an argument constraint; declare it on the owning table column", print::scalar_type(&ScalarType { kind, optional })).mechanical());
            }
            match &mut kind {
                TypeKind::Image { once, .. } => *once = true,
                TypeKind::Video { .. } => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns; video ranges are unspecified", "use photo: image <=10000000 once, or remove once from the video column")),
                TypeKind::File { .. } => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns; a downloaded file is saved by the browser, so one view cannot be kept", "remove once from the file column; use image <=10000000 once for one-view photos").guide("assets")),
                _ => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns", "remove once from this column; use image <=10000000 once for one-use images")),
            }
        }
        let optional = self.maybe("?");
        Ok(ScalarType { kind, optional })
    }

    pub(super) fn media_size(&mut self) -> Fallible<u32> {
        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
        let size = self.number()?;
        let multiplier = match self.peek().value.as_str() { "KB" => 1_000, "MB" => 1_000_000, "GB" => 1_000_000_000, _ => return Ok(size) };
        self.position += 1;
        size.checked_mul(multiplier).ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&site, "upload bound exceeds 4,294,967,295 bytes", "use image <=5MB or a byte count fitting u32").guide("types"))
    }
}

    fn select_index(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, mode: ReadMode, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, ast::IndexDecl, String)> {
        // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — retain bound keys for type selection and final lowering.
        // No cloned expression pass: nested lookups must not multiply binder work.
        let mut keys = Vec::<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>::new();
        let mixed = read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_some()) && read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_none());
        let types = if mixed {
            let prefix = read.keys.iter().take_while(|key| key.column.is_none()).count();
            self.bind_key_parts(info, read, prefix, &mut keys, out)?;
            Some(keys.iter().map(|(_, expr, ty)| (ty.clone(), matches!(expr, Expr::Literal(Value::String(_))))).collect::<Vec<_>>())
        } else { None };
        let mut normalized = super::mixed_keys::read_typed(info, read, self.catalog, types.as_deref())?;
        self.name_bound_keys(info, &normalized, &mut keys)?;
        let read = normalized.as_ref();
        super::validate_key_names(read)?;
        let mut positional_reason = None;
        if !read.keys.is_empty() && read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_none()) {
            let declared = |index: &&ast::IndexDecl| !self.catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone()));
            let mut candidates = info.indexes.iter().filter(declared).filter(|index| match &read.index {
                Some(name) => &index.name == name,
                None => index.unique && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len(),
            }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
            if read.index.is_none() && candidates.len() > 1 {
                self.bind_key_parts(info, read, read.keys.len(), &mut keys, out)?;
                let types = keys.iter().map(|(_, expr, ty)| (ty.clone(), matches!(expr, Expr::Literal(Value::String(_))))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                candidates = super::positional_keys::candidates(info, read, &types, &self.catalog.synthesized);
            }
            if read.index.is_none() && mode == ReadMode::Range && read.order_keys.is_empty() && candidates.len() == 1 {
                let scan = info.indexes.iter().filter(declared).find(|index| !index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() > read.keys.len());
                let columns = scan.map(|index| index.columns.as_slice()).unwrap_or(&candidates[0].columns);
                let predicate = read.keys.iter().zip(columns).map(|(key, column)| format!(".{column} = {}", self.catalog.source.operand(&key.value))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN", "a positional key selects one declared unique row, not a scan prefix",
                    format!("{} where {predicate} first 20 by .id", read.table)).guide("reads"));
            }
            if candidates.len() != 1 {
                return Err(super::positional_keys::refusal(info, read, &candidates, self.catalog));
            }
            if read.index.is_none() {
                positional_reason = Some(if info.indexes.iter().filter(declared).filter(|index| index.unique && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len()).count() == 1 {
                    "only declared unique key of this arity"
                } else { "unique positional key selected by argument types" });
                normalized.to_mut().index = Some(candidates[0].name.clone());
            }
        }
        let read = normalized.as_ref();
        let point = mode != ReadMode::Range;
        self.bind_key_parts(info, read, read.keys.len(), &mut keys, out)?;
        let named = keys.iter().all(|(c, _, _)| c.is_some()) && !keys.is_empty();
        let mut selection = if read.index.is_some() { "explicit by" } else if named { "named equality columns" } else { "compatible positional key types" };
        // A `words(..)` index serves one read: `matching` with exactly its
        // leading columns as keys. Every other read sees plain indexes only.
        let searching = mode == ReadMode::Range && read.matching.is_some();
        let mut candidates: Vec<&ast::IndexDecl> = info
            .indexes
            .iter()
            .filter(|index| read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|explicit| explicit == &index.name))
            .filter(|index| read.seeks.iter().all(|b| index.columns.get(keys.len()) == Some(&b.column)))
            .filter(|index| if searching { index.searches_after(keys.len()) } else { index.words.is_none() })
            .filter(|index| match mode {
                ReadMode::Point | ReadMode::FilteredPoint => index.unique && (index.columns.len() == keys.len() || (named && mode == ReadMode::FilteredPoint && info.total_unique(index) && index.columns.len() < keys.len())),
                ReadMode::Exists => (index.columns.len() == keys.len() && (named || read.index.is_some() || index.unique)) || (named && info.total_unique(index) && index.columns.len() < keys.len()),
                ReadMode::Range => index.columns.len() > keys.len() || (!read.order_keys.is_empty() && index.unique && index.columns.len() == keys.len()),
            })
            .filter(|index| {
                if named {
                    if index.columns.len() < keys.len() { index.columns.iter().all(|column| keys.iter().any(|(c, _, _)| c.as_ref() == Some(column))) } else { keys.iter().all(|(c, _, _)| index.columns[..keys.len()].contains(c.as_ref().unwrap())) }
                } else {
                    true
                }
            })
            .collect();
        if named && matches!(mode, ReadMode::FilteredPoint | ReadMode::Exists) {
            if let Some(width) = candidates.iter().filter(|i| info.total_unique(i)).map(|i| i.columns.len()).min() {
                candidates.retain(|i| info.total_unique(i) && i.columns.len() == width);
                // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.7 — named equalities covering two total unique keys
                // (`loans[borrower = viewer, telescope = t]`, lane 057) test one row either
                // way, the other key's columns being extra equalities on it; the first
                // declared key decides, as a rule probe already does (§11).
                // Same-column keys in two orders (`unique pair: low, high` and `unique
                // inverse: high, low`) still need an explicit `by` (guide indexes).
                let mut sets = candidates.iter().map(|index| { let mut columns = index.columns.clone(); columns.sort(); columns }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                sets.dedup();
                if candidates.len() > 1 && sets.len() == candidates.len() {
                    selection = "first declared unique key the named equalities cover";
                    candidates.truncate(1);
                }
            } else if candidates.iter().any(|i| i.unique) {
                // Exact nullable unique keys still select points. A competing
                // nonunique index must not turn an exists probe into a tie.
                candidates.retain(|i| i.unique);
            }
        }
        // Positional keys select by type; a text literal may meet an
        // identity column (it becomes an id once the index is chosen).
        if !named {
            candidates.retain(|index| {
                keys.iter().enumerate().all(|(position, (_, e, t))| {
                    index.columns.get(position).is_some_and(|column| {
                        let expected = self.key_ty(info, index, column);
                        t.comparable(&expected) || (expected.is_identity() && matches!(e, Expr::Literal(Value::String(_))))
                    })
                })
            });
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.5 — resolve remaining ambiguity without replacing an existing exact-type winner.
            let exact: Vec<_> = candidates.iter().copied().filter(|index| keys.iter().enumerate().all(|(position, (_, _, t))| index.columns.get(position).is_some_and(|column| self.key_ty(info, index, column) == t.required()))).collect();
//! A row-read verdict and field projection together authorize result pricing.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, ir::Read, meter::Meter,
    schema::{Effect, Index}, value::{Disclosed, Value}};

// Only successful row authorization in this module can mint this evidence.
pub(crate) struct RowAuthorization(());
pub(crate) struct AuthorizedRow<'a> {
    projection: Disclosed<'a>,
    authority: RowAuthorization,
    // @ref LLP 3000.014#5 — a row's witness deadline is provisional until an answer emits it.
    expiry: super::expiry::Pending,
}
impl AuthorizedRow<'_> {
    pub(super) fn expiry(&self) -> super::expiry::Pending { self.expiry }
    pub(super) fn scalar_heap(&self, field: &str) -> u64 { self.projection.scalar_heap(field, &self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn heap_bytes(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.heap_bytes(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn projection_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.projection_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn materialization_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.materialization_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn into_value(self) -> Value { self.projection.into_value() }
    pub(super) fn into_parts(self) -> (Value, RowAuthorization) { (self.projection.into_value(), self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn charge(self, meter: &mut Meter) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        meter.heap(self.heap_bytes() + self.projection_heap() + self.materialization_heap())?;
        Ok(self.into_value())
    }
}

impl RowAuthorization {
    pub(super) fn project(self, projection: Disclosed<'_>) -> AuthorizedRow<'_> { AuthorizedRow { projection, authority:self, expiry: Default::default() } }
}

impl<'r> Runner<'r> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — member reads share the row-read and field-disclosure capability.
    pub(super) fn authorized_member(&mut self, name: &str, id: &str, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] trusted: bool) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // @ref LLP 3000.024#decision — this id came from an authorized head in
        // this read-only transaction. Price its public image before decoding,
        // then recheck the rule with a separate witness reservation. Neither
        // hidden/expired images nor private fields can select actual-size admission.
        #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
        if trusted && !self.device && !table.read_lifetime_sensitive() && table.recorded.is_empty()
            && table.through.is_none() && table.written_by.values().all(|a|a.audience) {
            if !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)? { return Ok(None); }
            self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::text(id) + crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<Value>(1))?;
            let key = [Value::Id(id.into())];
            let Some((Some(row),_)) = self.scan_authorized(name,crate::schema::BY_ID,&key,&Default::default(),crate::rows::Dir::Asc,1,true,true,None)?.pop() else { return Ok(None); };
            let materialization = row.materialization_heap();
            let prepay = self.meter.mark();
            self.meter.heap(materialization)?;
            let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
            let row = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes,super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read),
                )], |runner,_| {
                let row = runner.authorize_image(name,row.into_value())?;
                if let Some(row) = &row { runner.next_expiry.inherit(row.expiry()); }
                Ok(row)
            });
            // The value now owns the default. Transfer its prepay to charge(),
            // keeping only one retained reservation after the recheck.
            self.meter.release_row_heap(prepay, materialization);
            let row = row?;
            if row.is_some() { expiry.emit(); }
            return row.map(|row|row.charge(&mut self.meter)).transpose();
        }
        let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table).saturating_add(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read));
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let row = self.authorized_scratch(bytes, &[(MeterKind::Examined, 1), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read),
                )], |runner, scope| {
            let Some(mut row) = runner.tx.get(scope, name, id)? else { return Ok(None); };
            crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
            let until = table.deadline(&row);
            let authorized = runner.authorize_image(name, row)?;
            if let Some(row) = &authorized { runner.next_expiry.inherit(row.expiry()); runner.next_expiry.note_option(until); }
            Ok(authorized)
        })?;
        if row.is_some() { expiry.emit(); }
        row.map(|row|row.charge(&mut self.meter)).transpose()
    }

    pub(super) fn authorize_image(&mut self, name: &str, row: Value) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a denied or expired candidate's witness deadlines are discarded with its scope.
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        if self.inactive(name, &row)? { return Ok(None); }
        if !self.check_rule(name, Effect::Read, None, &row)? {
            self.hidden_row();
            return Ok(None);
        }
        // Memory deadlines are hidden by Disclosed itself; transfer the live
        // boundary before projecting, still inside this candidate's expiry scope.
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() { self.note_expiry(table, &row); }
        let projection = self.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query();
        let visible = projection.into_value();
        Ok(Some(AuthorizedRow { projection: self.disclosed_row(table, visible).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: expiry.defer() }))
    }

    pub(super) fn scan_disclosed(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.scan_authorized(name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit,true,true,#[cfg(feature = "server-search")] None)
    }

    pub(super) fn scan_authorized(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool, trusted: bool, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields: Option<&[String]>) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {

            let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(rows.len());

    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — row and field authorization precede result pricing.
    pub(super) fn authorized_point(&mut self, read: &Read, index: &Index, key: &[Value], prepaid: bool, trusted: bool, prepaid_examined: bool, absent: &mut bool) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[&read.table];
        let row = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(table) {
            if !prepaid_examined && !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)? { self.point_cut(read, key); return Ok(None); }
            let scratch = self.meter.mark();

                    runner.tx.scan_metered(scope,&read.table,&read.index,key,&Default::default(),crate::rows::Dir::Asc,1,&mut runner.meter)

            // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a hidden, expired or absent candidate's deadlines are discarded with this scope.
            let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();

                    runner.point_miss(read, key);

 succeeded in 0ms:
**The emitter and the comparison.** A Lean emitter in `snapback4-lang`
(beside `emit/rust.rs`) embeds programs in the core fragment, and reports
any other program as unsupported. The Stage 1 harness gains the Lean model
as a further oracle on that fragment. The model is not normative: when it
and the engine disagree, the disagreement is classified as a Rust defect, a
model defect, or an open language question, and an open question comes to
Charlie with both behaviours stated.

**Widening.** The model grows to a further area (the meter, expiry,
ephemeral tables, maintained columns or residency) only when that area has
been stable long enough for the model to keep up. This record leaves "long
enough" to a later revision, which should measure it from the area's own
commit history.

**Status (2026-10-08): the model and its proofs are built.** The model is a
Lake project at `snapback4/lean/`, pinned to Lean 4.34.1, with no Mathlib.
`./check.sh` runs `lake build` (about 15 s cold). It refuses any `sorry`,
`admit`, `native_decide` or `axiom`, audits 38 headline theorems for
standard axioms only, and runs 30 hand-written examples from the guide
cards. All 30 agree.

The model was written under the independence rule. It is an executable
big-step semantics of the core fragment:

- values and their one order;
- expressions, with short-circuit evaluation, null assertions and helpers;
- point reads, pages with `complete` and `next`, `count` and `exists`;
- the four rules, with exact-key `exists` probes;
- insert, upsert, update and delete, with their refusal codes.

Its only meter is an abstract ceiling on the visible candidates examined.

**What is proved:**

- the value order is total, and agrees with an order-preserving key encoding
  (`encKey_agrees`);
- scan order is determined by key order (`pageRead_perm`);
- noninterference: a query's whole outcome is a function of the viewer's
  visible rows (`query_noninterference`). Pages return only readable rows
  (`page_rows_readable`), `count` is exactly the number of visible matches
  (`countRead_eq`), and a hidden insert changes no query
  (`query_ignores_hidden_insert`);
- write authority, per write and per mutation (`applyWrite_authorized`,
  `mutation_authorized`);
- completeness soundness (`pageRead_complete`);
- oracle 2 as a theorem: a cut page's rows, followed by its continuation's,
  equal the uncut page (`cut_continuation`);
- the two LLP 3000.003 shapes (`or_true_skips`, `identity_sum`);
- sound Hoare rules for every statement form.

Four planted model faults each broke the build.

`snapback4/lean/OPEN.md` records 30 open points, with the model's choice
for each. The engine has not been consulted on any of them. The points most
likely to disagree with the engine are:

- whether rule probes see hidden rows (R1);
- which images an unnamed `update` rule judges (R2);
- where null sorts (V2);
- `next` on an exactly filled page (C2);
- the precedence among refusal codes (W1–W4).

**Status (2026-10-08): the emitter and the Lean oracle are built.**

- **The emitter** (`snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean.rs`) translates a checked
  program's surface tree, not the binder's IR, so the binder stays under
  test. It takes the few types it needs (literal typing beside ids,
  positional keys) from the declarations the checker accepted. Anything
  outside the fragment is a named unsupported reason, never a partial
  translation. `snapback4 check --emit-lean <file.q>` prints one file.
- **The runner** is `quarry-oracle` (`snapback4/lean/Oracle.lean`): one JSON
  case per line in, one tagged outcome per line out, one process per worker.
  The model's semantics were not changed for I/O.
- **The oracle** is `snapback4-difftest run-lean`. It compares the engine
  (SQLite, default ceilings) with the model on kind, refusal code, tagged
  data, `complete` and `next` presence. Each side then continues from its own
  cursor, and a mutation's `observe` reread runs on each side's resulting
  store. Meter fields and sites are not compared. Lean-only generator
  variants (`lean::generate`) aim at `OPEN.md`: per-effect and named update
  rules, a probe target hidden by its read rule, nullable and id orders, and
  residual filters.
- **The nightly runner** builds `quarry-oracle` before its campaigns and

//! A request scope plus a response-layer backstop, never a caller channel.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — missing instrumentation must supersede older evidence.
use super::{Recent, Sites};
use serde_json::{Value, json};
use snapback4_core::{Context, engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace};
use std::{cell::RefCell, sync::{Arc, atomic::Ordering}};

thread_local! { static CURRENT: RefCell<Option<Evidence>> = const { RefCell::new(None) }; }
struct Evidence {
    recent: Recent,
    operation: String,
    request: u64,
    query: bool,
    trace: RefusalTrace,
    sites: Arc<Sites>,
    generation: Option<u64>,
}
pub(in crate::serve) struct RequestScope;
impl Drop for RequestScope { fn drop(&mut self) { CURRENT.with(|c| c.borrow_mut().take()); } }
pub(in crate::serve) fn begin(shared: &crate::serve::Shared, path: &str) -> Option<RequestScope> {
    if !shared.personas { return None; }
    let recent = shared.refusals.clone();
    let request = recent.requests.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed) + 1;
    CURRENT.with(|c| *c.borrow_mut() = Some(Evidence { recent,
        operation: path.strip_prefix("/q/").or_else(|| path.strip_prefix("/m/")).unwrap_or(path).into(),
        request, query: path.starts_with("/q/"), trace: Default::default(), sites: Default::default(), generation: None }));
    Some(RequestScope)
}
pub(in crate::serve) fn context(context: Context, sites: Arc<Sites>, generation: u64) -> Context {
    CURRENT.with(|c| {
        let mut c = c.borrow_mut();
        let Some(evidence) = c.as_mut() else { return context };
        evidence.sites = sites;
        evidence.generation = Some(generation);
        context.with_refusal_trace(evidence.trace.clone())
    })
}
pub(in crate::serve) struct Pending { recent: Recent, record: Value }
impl Drop for Pending {
    fn drop(&mut self) { self.recent.submit(std::mem::take(&mut self.record)); }
}
// Inspect the actual encoded envelope, after overflow substitution. This covers
// every responder using the shared JSON response layer, including receipt replay,
// static admission and future paths with no attached collector. No user data is
// copied into the record. Unknown empty answers enter only the opt-in local
// trace; successful visibility filtering is never a refusal.
pub(in crate::serve) fn response(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<Pending> {
    CURRENT.with(|c| {
        let mut c = c.borrow_mut();
        let evidence = c.as_mut()?;
        let body: Value = serde_json::from_slice(bytes).ok()?;
        if evidence.query && evidence.trace.hidden() > 0 {
            eprintln!("dev query: {} hidden: {} (encountered row images filtered by read rules)", evidence.operation, evidence.trace.hidden());
        }
        let code = body.get("denied").or_else(|| body.get("why")).and_then(|v| v.get("code")).and_then(Value::as_str);
        let traced = evidence.trace.take();
        let why = body.get("denied").or_else(|| body.get("why"));
        // The one filtered read that is evidence about a program refusal: the
        // refusing `t[x] ! else CODE` lookup itself, whose read rule hid the row.
        let hidden = traced.as_ref().filter(|cause| !cause.terminal && cause.phase == "read rule").and_then(|cause| {
            let site = why?.get("site")?.as_str()?;
            evidence.sites.contains_key(&(cause.table.clone(), format!("lookup {site}"))).then(|| (cause.table.clone(), evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "read rule".into())).cloned()))
        });
        let cause = traced.filter(|cause| {
            // A filtered read earlier in a program is not evidence about a
            // later uninstrumented terminal refusal in that same request.
            code.is_none() || cause.terminal
        });
        let empty_answer = code.is_none();
        let code = match code {
            Some(code) => code,
            None if evidence.query && body.get("data").is_some() && body.get("loading") != Some(&Value::Bool(true)) => {
                // Visibility filtering is a successful read, not a refusal.
                if cause.as_ref().is_some_and(|cause| !cause.terminal && !cause.no_candidate) { return None; }
                if evidence.recent.trace_empty && empty(&body["data"]) { "EMPTY_READ" }
                else { return None; }
            }
            None => return None,
        };
        let preflight = code == "E_AUTH" && evidence.generation.is_none() && why.and_then(|v| v.get("site")).is_none();
        let mut record = json!({"preflight":preflight,"operation":evidence.operation, "code":code, "requestId":evidence.request,
            "generation":evidence.generation, "clause":"cause not instrumented", "at":snapback4_core::now_ms()});
        // A program's own `require … else CODE` (or `! else CODE`) carries
        // its source site in the refusal itself: the response's own
        // evidence, not an older trace, so the log can name the line.
        if cause.is_none() && let Some(why) = why
            && why.get("rule").and_then(Value::as_str) == Some(code) && let Some(site) = why.get("site").and_then(Value::as_str) {
            record["clause"] = json!("program refusal");
            record["site"] = json!(site);
            record["siteKind"] = json!("program");
            if let Some((table, rule)) = &hidden {
                record["hiddenBy"] = json!({"table": table, "readRuleSite": rule});
            }
        }
        if empty_answer {
            record["emptyRead"] = json!(true);
            record["cause"] = json!(if cause.as_ref().is_some_and(|cause| cause.no_candidate) { "no-candidate" } else { "unrecorded" });
            record["clause"] = json!("no probe miss and no rule cause were recorded");
        }
        if let Some(cause) = cause {
            let clause = evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), cause.phase.clone()))
                .or_else(|| evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), cause.clause.clone())));
            let site = clause.or_else(|| evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "table".into())));
            // Whether the site is the clause's own line or the table's (an omitted rule).
            record["siteKind"] = json!(if clause.is_some() { "clause" } else if site.is_some() { "table" } else { "" });

+/// Wave-11 contextual reference family. Keep the sources independent of IR;
+/// the explicit spelling is a metamorphic oracle for the contextual spelling.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — inserted/lookup snapshots project only in reference contexts.
+pub fn qdiag(seed:u64) -> (Case,String,String) {
+    let mut case=base(seed);
+    case.program.body=Body::Block(Block { stmts:vec![],ret:Expr::Bool(true) });
+    case.program.prelude.clear(); case.steps.clear(); case.rows.clear(); case.budget=Ceilings::default();
+    case.args=json!({"key":"new-parent","k2":"new-child","bucket":0,"c":null,"n":null,"a":0,"b":1});
+    let mut rng=Rng::new(seed);
+    let (owner,visible)=(if rng.pick(2)==0 {"alice"} else {"bob"},rng.pick(2)==0);
+    if rng.pick(3)==0 { case.rows.push(("kids".into(),json!({"id":"old-child","item":"new-parent","owner":owner}))); }
+    let read=if visible {"allow"} else {".owner = viewer"};
+    let implicit=format!("table items:\n  sibling: items ? on delete keep\n  unique sibling\n  owner: principal\n  label: text <=64\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  item: items on delete keep\n  owner: principal\n  unique item\n  read <- {read}\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\nmutation run(key: id, k2: id, bucket: int, c: cursor ?, n: int ?, a: int, b: int):\n  item = insert items {{ id:key, owner:viewer, label:'parent' }}\n  insert kids {{ id:k2, item, owner:viewer }}\n  seen = items[items[key] ! else NOT_FOUND] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  return seen.id\nquery observe(key: id, k2: id):\n  return {{ item:items[key], child:kids[k2] }}\n");
+    let explicit=implicit.replace("id:k2, item, owner","id:k2, item:item.id, owner").replace("items[items[key] ! else NOT_FOUND]","items[(items[key] ! else NOT_FOUND).id]");
+    (case,implicit,explicit)
+}

+use snapback4_difftest::{generate,execute,lean};
+#[test]
+fn generated_contextual_sources_match_native_and_lean(){
+    for seed in 0..32 {
+        let (case,implicit,explicit)=generate::qdiag(seed);
+        let a=execute::execute(&mut execute::engine(&case,&implicit).unwrap(),&case,"run",&case.args);
+        let b=execute::execute(&mut execute::engine(&case,&explicit).unwrap(),&case,"run",&case.args);
+        assert_eq!(a,b,"seed {seed}");
+        for source in [&implicit,&explicit] {
+            let checked=lean::compare(&case,source).unwrap();
+            assert!(matches!(checked.verdict,snapback4_difftest::oracle::Verdict::Pass),"seed {seed}: {:?}",checked.verdict);
+        }
+    }
+}

mod common;
use common::*;
use snapback4_core::{Context, WriteState};
use snapback4_device::{device::Device,store::DeviceStore};
use serde_json::json;
const SOURCE:&str="table media:\n  photo: image <=5MB\n  public\ntable parents:\n  label: text <=20\n  unique label\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  unique parent\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\nfn label(p: parents): p.label\nmutation add(id: id, child: id, title: text <=20):\n  p = insert parents { id, label: title }\n  insert kids { id: child, parent: p }\n  return label(p)\nmutation clash(id: id, parent: parents):\n  insert parents { id: 'rolledback', label: 'temporary' }\n  insert kids { id, parent }\nquery parents(c: cursor ?):\n  return parents first 20 by .id after c\n";
fn run<H:Host>(host:&mut H){
 let (mut e,b)=compile(SOURCE);
 let mut d=host.open("qdiag",Some(b)).unwrap();
 for (i,page) in snapshot_pages(&mut e,"alice",10).iter().enumerate(){d.apply(page,i==0).unwrap();}
 let args=json!({"id":"p","child":"k","title":"label"});
 d.predict("add","alice",&args,100,vec![],1).unwrap();
 let actual=e.mutate("add",None,Context::new("alice",common::args(&[("id",id("p")),("child",id("k")),("title",snapback4_core::Value::String("label".into()))])).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(actual.state,WriteState::Sent { result:snapback4_core::Value::String(ref s),.. } if s=="label"),"{actual:?}");
 let row=e.store().rows("kids").unwrap().pop().unwrap();
 assert_eq!(row.field("parent").unwrap(),&id("p"));
 let predicted=d.predict("clash","alice",&json!({"id":"other","parent":"p"}),100,vec![],2).unwrap_err();
 assert_eq!(predicted.code(),"E_UNIQUE","{predicted:?}");
 let conflict=e.mutate("clash",None,Context::new("alice",common::args(&[("id",id("other")),("parent",id("p"))])).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(conflict.state,WriteState::Failed { ref why,.. } if why.code=="E_UNIQUE" && why.message.contains("unique parent") && !why.message.contains("$intent")),"{conflict:?}");
 assert_eq!(e.store().rows("parents").unwrap().len(),1,"the earlier write rolled back");
 assert_eq!(query(&mut d),json!([{"id":"p","label":"label","pending":true}]),"device refusal rolled back");
 host.close("qdiag",d);
}
fn query<S:DeviceStore>(d:&mut Device<S>)->serde_json::Value{snapback4_device::call::call(d,json!({"op":"query","name":"parents","viewer":"alice","args":{"c":null},"now":100}))["ok"]["data"].clone()}
#[test]fn qdiag_memory(){run(&mut Memory::default());}
#[test]fn qdiag_sqlite(){run(&mut Sqlite::new("wave11-qdiag"));}

fn engine(source:&str)->Engine{let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap()}
#[test]
fn contextual_projection_preserves_every_published_observation(){
 let source="table media:\n  photo: image <=5MB\n  public\ntable parents:\n  label: text <=20\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\nmutation create(id: id, kid: id):\n  parent = insert parents { id, label:'parent' }\n  insert kids { id:kid, parent }\n  return parent.id\nquery list(c: cursor ?):\n  return kids first 10 by .id after c\n";
 let mut a=engine(source);let mut b=engine(&source.replace("id:kid, parent","id:kid, parent:parent.id").replace("5MB","5000000"));
 let (_,a)=observation::observe(&mut a,"create","alice",json!({"id":"p","kid":"k"}),"qdiag-op",("list",json!({"c":null})));
 let (_,b)=observation::observe(&mut b,"create","alice",json!({"id":"p","kid":"k"}),"qdiag-op",("list",json!({"c":null})));
 a.assert_same(&b);
}
