//! The page candidate contract: a provisional deadline travels with its image
//! and is emitted only by the page. The scan loop inlines this under the
//! sealed projection boundary (`AuthorizedRow`, LLP 3000.015 §1); the
//! read_verdict tests exercise the contract itself.
use super::{expiry::Pending, runner::Runner};
use crate::{error::RuntimeError, schema::Effect, value::Value};

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn read_candidate(
        &mut self,
        table_name: &str,
        row: Value,
        uniform_verdict: Option<bool>,
        prefix_expiry: Pending,
    ) -> Result<Option<(Value, Pending)>, RuntimeError> {
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        self.next_expiry.inherit(prefix_expiry);
        let allowed = match uniform_verdict {
            Some(verdict) => verdict && self.provisional_readable(table_name, &row)?,
            None => self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, &row)?,
        };
        if self.read_capped() || !allowed {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        self.note_expiry(table, &row);
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.21 — an unfinished live total is unavailable.
        let row = self.live_aggregate(table_name, row)?;
        if self.read_capped() {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        // Authorization is not emission. Filters, lookahead and retention still
        // have to finish before the caller can commit this candidate's deadline.
        row.map(|mut row| {
            self.stamp_source(table_name, &mut row)?;
            Ok((super::recorded::public_image(table, row), expiry.defer()))
        }).transpose()
    }
}
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState, engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
fn id(s:&str)->Value{Value::Id(s.into())}
fn text(s:&str)->Value{Value::String(s.into())}
fn args(v:&[(&str,Value)])->BTreeMap<String,Value>{v.iter().map(|(k,v)|(k.to_string(),v.clone())).collect()}
fn engine(source:&str)->Engine{let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap()}
fn why(e:&mut Engine,op:&str,who:&str,values:&[(&str,Value)])->snapback4_core::Refusal{match e.mutate(op,None,Context::new(who,args(values)).at(100)).unwrap().state{WriteState::Failed {why,..}=>why,s=>panic!("{s:?}")}}
const UNIQUE:&str="table rows:\n  owner: principal\n  writer: principal\n  handle: text <=20\n  other: text <=20 ?\n  unique handle\n  unique other else OTHER_TAKEN 'Other key is taken'\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .writer = viewer\n  update <- .writer = viewer\nmutation add(id: id, owner: principal, writer: principal, handle: text <=20, other: text <=20 ?):\n  return insert rows {id,owner,writer,handle,other}\nmutation absent(id: id, owner: principal, writer: principal, handle: text <=20, other: text <=20 ?):\n  return insert rows {id,owner,writer,handle,other} if absent\nquery page(c: cursor ?):\n  return rows first 10 by .id after c\n";
fn seed(e:&mut Engine,id_:&str,owner:&str,handle:&str,other:Option<&str>){e.seed(vec![("rows".into(),snapback4_core::value::object([("id",id(id_)),("owner",id(owner)),("writer",id(owner)),("handle",text(handle)),("other",other.map(text).unwrap_or(Value::Null))]))]).unwrap();}
fn input(id_:&str,owner:&str,handle:&str,other:Option<&str>)->Vec<(&'static str,Value)>{vec![("id",id(id_)),("owner",id(owner)),("writer",id(owner)),("handle",text(handle)),("other",other.map(text).unwrap_or(Value::Null))]}
#[test]
fn visible_unique_names_the_declaration_and_authored_code_wins(){
 let mut e=engine(UNIQUE);seed(&mut e,"old","alice","key",Some("other"));
 let visible=why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("new","alice","key",None));
 assert_eq!(visible.code,"E_UNIQUE");assert!(visible.message.contains("unique handle") && !visible.message.contains("$intent"));
 let named=why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("new","alice","different",Some("other")));assert_eq!(named.code,"OTHER_TAKEN");
 assert_eq!(why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("old","alice","different",None)).code,"E_CONSTRAINT");
 let absent=e.mutate("absent",None,Context::new("alice",args(&input("new","alice","key",None))).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(absent.state,WriteState::Sent {result:Value::Null,..}));
 // Null secondary keys do not conflict; the handle remains independently unique.
 for (i,handle) in [("two","two"),("three","three")] {assert!(matches!(e.mutate("add",None,Context::new("alice",args(&input(i,"alice",handle,None))).at(100)).unwrap().state,WriteState::Sent {..}));}
}
#[test]
fn hidden_unique_is_the_same_public_refusal_as_rule_denial_including_seats(){
 for viewer in ["alice","service:ops"] {
  let mut e=engine(UNIQUE);seed(&mut e,"old","bob","key",None);
  let collision=why(&mut e,"add",viewer,&input("new",viewer,"key",None));
  let mut denied=input("new",viewer,"different",None);denied[2].1=id("other");
  let denial=why(&mut e,"add",viewer,&denied);
  assert_eq!(collision,denial,"{viewer}");assert_eq!(collision.code,"E_RULE");assert!(collision.message.contains("a row you cannot read holds this key"));
  if viewer.starts_with("service:"){assert!(collision.message.contains("rows rule clause: insert <- .writer = viewer"));}
  assert_eq!(e.store().rows("rows").unwrap().len(),1);
 }
}
#[test]
fn hidden_counter_is_request_local_and_changes_no_query_envelope_or_meter(){
 let mut e=engine(UNIQUE);seed(&mut e,"a","alice","a",None);seed(&mut e,"b","bob","b",None);
 let plain=e.query("page",Context::new("alice",args(&[("c",Value::Null)])).at(100)).unwrap();
 let trace=RefusalTrace::default();
 let traced=e.query("page",Context::new("alice",args(&[("c",Value::Null)])).at(100).with_refusal_trace(trace.clone())).unwrap();
 assert_eq!(serde_json::to_value(plain).unwrap(),serde_json::to_value(traced).unwrap());
 assert_eq!(trace.hidden(),1);
 let next=RefusalTrace::default();e.query("page",Context::new(snapback4_core::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL,args(&[("c",Value::Null)])).at(100).with_refusal_trace(next.clone())).unwrap();assert_eq!(next.hidden(),0);
}
#[test]
fn ephemeral_seat_denials_have_the_same_static_clause(){
 let mut e=engine("table typing:\n  owner: principal\n  unique owner\n  cap 50\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  ephemeral 4s\nmutation add(owner: principal):\n  return insert typing {owner}\n");
 let denied=why(&mut e,"add","service:ops",&[("owner",id("alice"))]);assert_eq!(denied.code,"E_RULE");assert!(denied.message.ends_with("typing rule clause: insert <- .owner = viewer"),"{denied:?}");
}
#[test]
fn hidden_point_images_are_counted_outside_the_query_envelope(){
 let source=format!("{UNIQUE}query point(key: rows):\n  return rows[key]\n");let mut e=engine(&source);seed(&mut e,"hidden","bob","key",None);
 let trace=RefusalTrace::default();let values=args(&[("key",id("hidden"))]);
 let plain=e.query("point",Context::new("alice",values.clone()).at(100)).unwrap();
 let traced=e.query("point",Context::new("alice",values).at(100).with_refusal_trace(trace.clone())).unwrap();assert_eq!(serde_json::to_value(plain).unwrap(),serde_json::to_value(traced).unwrap());assert_eq!(trace.hidden(),1);
}

//! Referential actions: what deleting a referenced row does to the rows that
//! name it (`on delete restrict | cascade | set null`).
// @ref LLP 3000.039#2 — every reference between ordinary durable tables carries one action; restrict is the default.
use super::*;
pub use reservations::Price;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub enum OnDelete {
    Restrict,
    Cascade,
    SetNull,
    /// The referencing row stays and names a deleted row (the pre-0.5 meaning),
    /// declared on purpose: history, audit trails, contributions that outlive
    /// their target.
    Keep,
}

impl OnDelete {
    pub fn words(self) -> &'static str {
        match self { Self::Restrict => "on delete restrict", Self::Cascade => "on delete cascade", Self::SetNull => "on delete set null", Self::Keep => "on delete keep" }
    }
    /// The actions that write the children, bounded by a cap on the reference.
    pub fn writes(self) -> bool { matches!(self, Self::Cascade | Self::SetNull) }
    /// The actions a delete must consult (every one but `keep`).
    pub fn acts(self) -> bool { self != Self::Keep }
}

/// One action-carrying reference `child.column -> parent`.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub struct Referencing<'a> {
    pub child: &'a str,
    pub table: &'a Table,
    pub column: &'a str,
    pub action: OnDelete,
}

/// The prefix of the reference index a live child of `parent` is found under.
pub const REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX: &str = "$reference:";
pub fn reference_index_name(column: &str) -> String { format!("{REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX}{column}") }

/// Heap a closure keeps per reached row: its table and id (an id is at most
/// 144 canonical bytes) and the set and queue entries holding them.
pub const CLOSURE_IDENTITY_BYTES: u64 = 384;
/// Child rows a cascade or set-null walk reads per seek (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
pub const REFERENCE_PAGE: usize = 4;

impl Schema {
    /// Whether rows of `name` take part in referential actions, as child or
    /// parent: an ordinary durable application table. Ephemeral rows, engine
    /// derivations and receipt/timeline bookkeeping keep their own lifetimes.
    pub fn actionable(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        let Some(table) = self.table(name) else { return false };
        table.ephemeral.is_none() && self.maintain_for(name).is_none() && self.private_owner(name).is_none()
            && !self.is_recount_work(name) && !self.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.owns(name))
            && !self.receipts.iter().any(|f| [&f.scope, &f.members, &f.runs].iter().any(|t| t.as_str() == name))
    }

    /// Every reference into `parent` a delete consults (all but `keep`), in
    /// (child, column) order.
    pub fn referencing<'a>(&'a self, parent: &str) -> Vec<Referencing<'a>> {
        // A plain loop into a vector: iterator adapters monomorphize per call site on the web device.
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for (child, table) in &self.tables {
            for (column, action) in &table.on_delete {
                if action.acts() && matches!(table.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: target }) if target == parent) {
                    out.push(Referencing { child, table, column, action: *action });
                }
            }
        }
        out
    }

    /// A delete of a `parent` row consults its referencing rows, so it needs
    /// read authority on that row (LLP 3000.039 §3 step 1).
    pub fn is_referenced(&self, parent: &str) -> bool { !self.referencing(parent).is_empty() }

    /// The index a reference is probed through: its column first, then the
    /// child's deadline column when the child can expire, so one seek skips an
    /// expired backlog. A `read through` reference uses its child index.
    pub fn reference_index(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        let table = self.table(child)?;
        if table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == column) {
            return table.indexes.contains_key(through::CHILDREN).then_some(through::CHILDREN);
        }
        // The effective deadline: a read-through child's `$deadline`, else `expire at`.
        let deadline = table.expiry_column();
        table.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| {
            let columns = index.columns();
            index.is_plain() && columns.first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
                && deadline.is_none_or(|deadline| columns.get(1).map(String::as_str) == Some(deadline))
        }).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str())
    }

    /// The cap bounding a reference's children: `cap N by <column>` exactly,
    /// or one when the reference itself is a plain unique key. A nullable
    /// reference may have many nulls, but a parent delete probes one non-null
    /// parent id, whose unique group still has at most one row.
    pub fn reference_cap(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<u32> {
        let table = self.table(child)?;
        let declared = table.constraints.iter().find_map(|c| match c {
            Constraint::Cap { by, max } if by.len() == 1 && by[0] == column => Some(*max),
            _ => None,
        });
        let unique = table.indexes.values().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns() == [column]).then_some(1);
        declared.into_iter().chain(unique).min()
    }

    /// Whether `cap` on `table` skips a group whose reference is null: only a
    /// `set null` reference's own cap does (a null reference has no parent).
    pub fn cap_skips_null(&self, table: &Table, by: &[String], group: &[Value]) -> bool {
        by.iter().zip(group).any(|(column, value)| *value == Value::Null && table.on_delete.get(column) == Some(&OnDelete::SetNull))
    }

    /// The tables a delete of a `parent` row may write through its writing
    /// actions, and the tables its actions read. A function of the schema, so
    /// a commit's touched set never says which of them a delete reached.
    pub fn action_tables(&self, parent: &str) -> ActionTables {
        let mut out = ActionTables::default();
        let mut queue = vec![parent.to_owned()];
        let mut visited = BTreeSet::new();
        while let Some(table) = queue.pop() {
            if !visited.insert(table.clone()) { continue; }
            for r in self.referencing(&table) {
                out.read.insert(r.child.to_owned());
                if r.action.writes() { out.written.insert(r.child.to_owned()); }
                if r.action == OnDelete::Cascade { queue.push(r.child.to_owned()); }
            }
        }
        // What a write of each of those tables writes in turn: the derived
        // destinations `row_changed` names (aggregate targets, maintained-column
        // owners, row-keyed views).
        let derived: Vec<String> = self.maintains.iter().filter(|m| out.written.contains(&m.source) || m.row_key.as_ref().is_some_and(|k| out.written.contains(&k.table)))
            .flat_map(|m| [Some(m.target.clone()), m.private.as_ref().map(|owner| owner.table.clone())]).flatten().collect();
        out.written.extend(derived);
        out.written.retain(|name| self.visible(name));
        // Private derivations disappear from the device's projection; their
        // event envelope is exported with each direct source's write price.
        let sources: Vec<String> = out.written.iter().cloned().collect();
        for source in sources {
            if let Some(events) = self.write_reservation(&source).and_then(|plan| plan.action_events) { out.written.extend(events.tables); }
        }
        out
    }

    /// The order a delete's closure is removed in: a row before any table it
    /// must not outlive — its cascade parent, and its `read through` parent.
    /// A cycle through those edges has no valid order and is refused.
    pub fn deletion_precedes(&self, child: &str, parent: &str) -> bool {
        self.table(child).is_some_and(|t| t.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|through| through.parent == parent))
            || self.referencing(parent).into_iter().any(|r| r.child == child && r.action == OnDelete::Cascade)
    }

    /// A table that reaches itself through cascade and `read through` edges.
    pub fn deletion_cycle(&self) -> Option<String> {
        fn visit<'a>(schema: &'a Schema, name: &'a str, path: &mut Vec<&'a str>, done: &mut BTreeSet<&'a str>) -> Option<String> {
            if path.contains(&name) { return Some(name.to_owned()); }
            if !done.insert(name) { return None; }
            path.push(name);
            for (child, _) in &schema.tables {
                if schema.deletion_precedes(child, name) && let Some(cycle) = visit(schema, child, path, done) { return Some(cycle); }
            }
            path.pop();
            None
        }
        let mut done = BTreeSet::new();
        self.tables.keys().find_map(|name| if self.tables[name].on_delete.values().any(|a| *a == OnDelete::Cascade) || self.is_referenced(name) {
            let mut path = Vec::new();
            visit(self, name, &mut path, &mut done)
        } else { None })
    }

    /// Whether `name` is a table a timeline orders the writes of.
    pub fn timeline_member(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        self.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.owns(name) || t.maps(name) || t.authors.first().is_some_and(|a| a == name))
    }

    /// The most rows of each table one delete of a `parent` row can reach
    /// through its cascades (its closure), the root included: each cascade
    /// edge multiplies by its cap, and edges into a table add up. `None` when
    /// the cascade graph from `parent` is cyclic or a cascade lacks its cap.
    pub fn closure_bounds(&self, parent: &str) -> Option<Vec<(String, u64)>> {
        // Vectors, not maps: the web device pays for every new collection type.
        let mut reach: Vec<String> = vec![parent.to_owned()];
        let mut edges: Vec<(usize, usize, u64)> = Vec::new();
        let mut at = 0;
        while at < reach.len() {
            let table = reach[at].clone();
            for r in self.referencing(&table) {
                if r.action != OnDelete::Cascade { continue; }
                if r.action != OnDelete::Cascade { continue; }
                let cap = u64::from(self.reference_cap(r.child, r.column)?);
                let child = reach.iter().position(|t| t == r.child).unwrap_or_else(|| { reach.push(r.child.to_owned()); reach.len() - 1 });
                edges.push((at, child, cap));
            }
            at += 1;
        }
        // Kahn's order over the reached cascade subgraph; a leftover table is a cycle.
        let mut indegree = vec![0_usize; reach.len()];
        for &(_, child, _) in &edges { indegree[child] += 1; }
        let mut bounds = vec![0_u64; reach.len()];
        bounds[0] = 1;
        let mut ready: Vec<usize> = (0..reach.len()).filter(|&t| indegree[t] == 0).collect();
        let mut done = 0;
        while let Some(table) = ready.pop() {
            done += 1;
            for &(from, child, cap) in edges.iter().filter(|(from, _, _)| *from == table) {
                bounds[child] = bounds[child].saturating_add(bounds[from].saturating_mul(cap));
                indegree[child] -= 1;
                if indegree[child] == 0 { ready.push(child); }
            }
        }
        (done == reach.len()).then(|| reach.into_iter().zip(bounds).collect())
    }

    /// The fixed charge a delete of a `parent` row reserves before it probes,
    /// cascades or clears any row, over every meter: the closure's rows and
    /// their identities, every restrict probe (one seek per closure row, plus
    /// the closure rows it skips), every set-null row, and one engine write of
    /// each, priced from the source-write plan the engine already exports. A
    /// function of the schema only — caps, the reference graph, each table's
    /// indexes and row size, and the exported plans — so a meter, an `E_BOUND`
    /// abort or the physical allowance never depends on how many (hidden) rows
    /// exist, and every host charges alike: a device's projection keeps every
    /// table that can carry an action and every exported price. `None` when the
    /// closure is unbounded (a cycle or a missing cap) or a written table has no
    /// fixed write price. Heap is the closure's identity scratch plus the largest
    /// single row's transient work, plus event images retained until commit.
    // @ref LLP 3000.039#5.1 — the reservation is schema-fixed; LLP 3000.015 §2's law for work shaped by hidden rows.
    pub fn delete_reservation(&self, parent: &str) -> Option<Price> {
        let bounds = self.closure_bounds(parent)?;
        let mut price = [0_u64; 7];
        let mut heap = 0_u64;
        let mut identities = 0_u64;
        let mut gathered = 0_u64;
        let rows_of = |name: &str| bounds.iter().find(|(t, _)| t == name).map_or(0, |(_, n)| *n);
        for (table, n) in &bounds {
            let n = *n;
            if table != parent {
                // Fetched by the closure walk and again, current, before its delete.
                let unit = self.unit_write(table)?;
                heap = heap.max(unit[4]);
                add(&mut price, [unit[0], unit[1], unit[2] + 1, unit[3], 0, unit[5], unit[6]].map(|v| v.saturating_mul(n)));
                identities = identities.saturating_add(n);
            }
            for r in self.referencing(table) {
                // A capped walk holds one page of child images per seek.
                let row = crate::engine::row_copy_ceiling(r.table);
                heap = heap.max(row.saturating_mul(REFERENCE_PAGE as u64));
                let seeks = if r.table.expiry_column().is_some() && r.table.through.as_ref().is_none_or(|t| t.reference != r.column) { 2 } else { 1 };
                // A walk over a capped group: a seek per page, and a last, short page per range.
                let walk = |cap: u64| seeks * n + cap.saturating_mul(n) / REFERENCE_PAGE as u64;
                // Every reached row runs the lifetime hook for every through
                // child, including children the closure already cascaded away.
                let through = r.table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == r.column);
                if through && table != parent { add(&mut price, [3_u64, 0, 3, 0, 0, 0, 0].map(|v| v.saturating_mul(n))); }
                match r.action {
                    // A read-through child's probe: three seeks per closure row (LLP 3000.014 §6).
                    OnDelete::Restrict if through => {},
                    // One probe per closure row, a row per seek: it may skip the closure
                    // rows that name it (each names one target per reference), then stops
                    // at its first outside witness.
                    OnDelete::Restrict => {
                        let probe = seeks * n + rows_of(r.child) + n;
                        add(&mut price, [2 * probe, probe, seeks * n + rows_of(r.child) + n, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
                    }
                    OnDelete::Cascade => {
                        let pages = walk(u64::from(self.reference_cap(r.child, r.column)?));
                        add(&mut price, [2 * pages, pages, seeks * n, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
                    }
                    OnDelete::SetNull => {
                        let cap = u64::from(self.reference_cap(r.child, r.column)?);
                        gathered = gathered.max(cap);
                        let cleared = cap.saturating_mul(n);
                        let unit = self.unit_write(r.child)?;
                        heap = heap.max(unit[4]);
                        let pages = walk(cap);
                        add(&mut price, [2 * pages, pages, seeks * n, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
                        add(&mut price, [unit[0], unit[1], unit[2] + 1, unit[3], 0, unit[5], unit[6]].map(|v| v.saturating_mul(cleared)));
                    }
                    OnDelete::Keep => {}
                }
            }
        }
        // The root is read again, current, before its own removal.
        price[2] = price[2].saturating_add(1);
        // The closure's identities (table and id) are held until the last
        // delete, with the root's and one page of child ids.
        // A set-null group is gathered whole (at most its cap) before it is cleared.
        price[4] = heap.saturating_add(identities.saturating_add(1 + REFERENCE_PAGE as u64 + gathered).saturating_mul(CLOSURE_IDENTITY_BYTES));
        let mut receiver_steps = 0;
        for (table, _) in &bounds {
            for reference in self.referencing(table) {
                if reference.action.writes() { receiver_steps = receiver_steps.max(self.write_reservation(reference.child)?.action_events?.steps_per_write); }
            }
        }
        price[0] = price[0].saturating_add(price[3].saturating_mul(receiver_steps));
        price[4] = price[4].saturating_add(self.retained_action_heap(parent, price[3])?);
        Some(price)
    }

    /// Diagnostic recipe price: action work plus the root's ordinary write.
    /// Execution retains its existing separate charges for these two terms.
    pub fn delete_total_reservation(&self, parent: &str) -> Option<Price> {
        let mut total = self.delete_reservation(parent)?;
        add(&mut total, self.unit_write(parent)?);
        Some(total)
    }

    /// Event images remain live until commit, after action scratch is released.
    pub fn delete_event_heap(&self, parent: &str) -> Option<u64> {
        self.retained_action_heap(parent, self.delete_reservation(parent)?[3])
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.039#5.1 — two event images per priced row write, plus bounded keys and audience transitions, retained for the request lifetime.
    fn retained_action_heap(&self, parent: &str, writes: u64) -> Option<u64> {
        let mut per_write = 0;
        for (table, _) in self.closure_bounds(parent)? {
            for reference in self.referencing(&table) {
                if reference.action.writes() {
                    let events = self.write_reservation(reference.child)?.action_events?;
                    per_write = per_write.max(events.heap_per_write);
                }
            }
        }
        Some(writes.saturating_mul(per_write))
    }

    /// One engine write (delete or set-null update) of a `table` row: fetch and
    /// write it, its index entries (a `words(..)` index at its token bound), a
    /// row copy, and the source-write price the engine exports for the table.
    fn unit_write(&self, name: &str) -> Option<Price> {
        let table = self.table(name)?;
        let plan = self.write_reservation(name)?;
        if plan.server_only { return None; }
        let entries = table.indexes.values().map(|index| match index.word_component() {
            Some((_, fields)) => fields.iter().filter_map(|f| table.columns.get(f)).map(|kind| match kind.required() {
                ColumnType::Text { max } => u64::from(*max), _ => 0,
            }).sum::<u64>().min(crate::words::MAX_TOKENS_PER_ROW as u64),
            None => 1,
        }).fold(0_u64, u64::saturating_add);
        let row = crate::engine::row_copy_ceiling(table);
        // The row copy and every phase's heap are held together until the
        // write's attempt ends (an asset phase beside a column recount): a sum.
        let mut unit = [4 + 2 * entries, 2 * entries, 1, 1, row.saturating_mul(4), 0, 0];
        for phase in 0..=reservations::VIEW_READ {
            add(&mut unit, plan.price(phase));
        }
        // The lifetime hook reserves all child-table witnesses while source
        // phases are still held. Device verification additionally holds the
        // parent and a child scratch image; every host prices the same peak.
        let mut lifetime = 0_u64;
        let mut child_peak = 0_u64;
        for (_, child, _) in self.children(name) {
            let scratch = crate::engine::row_copy_ceiling(child).saturating_mul(3);
            lifetime = lifetime.saturating_add(scratch);
            child_peak = child_peak.max(scratch);
        }
        if lifetime > 0 { unit[4] = unit[4].saturating_add(lifetime).saturating_add(child_peak).saturating_add(row); }
        Some(unit)
    }

//! One logical allowance for work whose shape can depend on unreadable rows.
use super::runner::Runner;
use std::cell::{Cell, RefCell};

// @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — one physical allowance per request, nested work included.
pub(super) const AUTHORIZATION_WALK_LIMIT: usize = 8192;
use crate::{
    assets::Asset,
    error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{AcquiredBoundary, Bounds, Dir, PrefetchRequest, Rows}, schema::{Index, Table}, value::Value,
};

// Only this module mints the capability; it cannot be constructed or cloned
// by callers, and the borrowed token cannot escape the guarded closure.
pub struct Authorization { probes: Cell<u64>, remaining_probes: u64 }

impl Authorization {
    fn internal() -> Self { Self { probes: std::cell::Cell::new(0), remaining_probes: u64::MAX } }
    // The candidate's declared probe allowance is checked before authorization
    // executes. The enclosing scope commits this logical count on every exit.
    pub(super) fn admit_probes(&self, probes: u64) -> bool {
        let total = self.probes.get().saturating_add(probes);
        self.probes.set(total);
        total <= self.remaining_probes
    }

}


// No Rows implementation, Deref or raw accessor: row reads require the
// unforgeable token even when a caller has access to Runner's store facade.
pub(crate) struct GuardedRows<'a> { raw: &'a dyn Rows, remaining: Cell<usize>, authorizing: Cell<bool>, reference_prepaid: Cell<bool>, revision: Cell<u64>, lookup_cache: RefCell<Option<(String, String, Vec<Value>, Option<Value>)>> }
impl<'a> GuardedRows<'a> {
    pub(crate) fn new(raw: &'a dyn Rows) -> Self { Self { raw, remaining: Cell::new(AUTHORIZATION_WALK_LIMIT), authorizing: Cell::new(false), reference_prepaid: Cell::new(false), revision: Cell::new(0), lookup_cache: RefCell::new(None) } }
    pub(super) fn revision(&self) -> u64 { self.revision.get() }
    pub(super) fn remaining(&self) -> usize { self.remaining.get() }
    fn limit(&self, table: &str, requested: usize) -> Result<usize, RuntimeError> {
        if !self.authorizing.get() || self.reference_prepaid.get() { return Ok(requested); }
        if self.remaining.get() == 0 { return Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Read }); }
        Ok(requested.min(self.remaining.get()))
    }
    fn charge(&self, fetched: usize) {
        if self.authorizing.get() && !self.reference_prepaid.get() { self.remaining.set(self.remaining.get().saturating_sub(fetched)); }
    }
    /// Return the walk allowance of a fetched row the store stepped over as
    /// expired: such rows are absent, so they cannot exhaust a read's walk.
    pub(super) fn refund(&self, fetched: usize) {
        if self.authorizing.get() { self.remaining.set(self.remaining.get() + fetched); }
    }
    pub(crate) fn get(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, id: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let row = self.raw.get(table, id)?;
        self.charge(usize::from(row.is_some()));
        Ok(row)
    }
    /// A key-only witness still consumes the shared physical authorization allowance.
    pub(crate) fn index_head(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir) -> Result<Option<Vec<Value>>, RuntimeError> {
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let key = self.raw.index_head(table, index, prefix, bounds, dir)?;
        self.charge(usize::from(key.is_some()));
        Ok(key)
    }
    pub(crate) fn lookup(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, index: &Index, key: &[Value]) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — repeated unchanged points share decoded storage,
        // while every logical read still prepays its complete rule.
        if let Some((cached_table, cached_index, cached_key, row)) = &*self.lookup_cache.borrow()
            && cached_table == table && cached_index == name && cached_key == key { return Ok(row.clone()); }
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let row = self.raw.lookup(table, name, index, key)?;
        self.charge(usize::from(row.is_some()));
        *self.lookup_cache.borrow_mut() = Some((table.into(), name.into(), key.to_vec(), row.clone()));
        Ok(row)
    }
    pub(crate) fn scan(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let permitted = self.limit(table, limit)?;
        let rows = self.raw.scan(table, name, prefix, bounds, dir, permitted)?;
        self.charge(rows.len());
        if permitted < limit && rows.len() == permitted { return Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Read }); }
        Ok(rows)
    }
    #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
    pub(crate) fn scan_selected(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir, fields: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let rows = self.raw.scan_selected(table, name, prefix, bounds, dir, fields)?;
        self.charge(rows.len());
        Ok(rows)
    }
    pub(crate) fn scan_metered(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir, limit: usize, meter: &mut Meter) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let permitted = self.limit(table, limit)?;
        let rows = InternalLedger::with_meter(meter, |meter, _| {
            self.raw.scan_metered(table, name, prefix, bounds, dir, permitted, _authorization, meter)
        })?;
        self.charge(rows.len());
        if permitted < limit && rows.len() == permitted { return Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Read }); }
        Ok(rows)
    }
    pub(crate) fn count_prefix(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], limit: u64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        // This scalar adapter is used only by non-expiring, non-private
        // caps. It fetches no candidate rows and keeps its declared cap bound.
        self.raw.count_prefix(table, name, prefix, limit)
    }

    pub(crate) fn cap_count(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, by: &[String], group: &[Value]) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> { self.raw.cap_count(table, by, group) }

    pub(crate) fn sequence_value(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, parent: &str, id: &str) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> { self.raw.sequence_value(parent, id) }
    pub(crate) fn provisional_sequence_floor(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, parent: &str, id: &str) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> { self.raw.provisional_sequence_floor(parent, id) }
    pub(crate) fn set_sequence(&self, parent: &str, id: &str, value: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> { self.raw.set_sequence(parent, id, value) }
    pub(crate) fn put(&self, name: &str, table: &Table, row: &Value, old: Option<&Value>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> { self.revision.set(self.revision.get().wrapping_add(1)); self.lookup_cache.borrow_mut().take(); self.raw.put(name, table, row, old) }
    pub(crate) fn delete(&self, name: &str, table: &Table, old: &Value) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> { self.revision.set(self.revision.get().wrapping_add(1)); self.lookup_cache.borrow_mut().take(); self.raw.delete(name, table, old)
    }
    pub(crate) fn track_time_audience(
        &self,
        name: &str,
        table: &Table,
        id: &str,
        row: Option<&Value>,
        now: i64,
        published: bool,
    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.raw.track_time_audience(name, table, id, row, now, published)
    }
    pub(crate) fn time_audience_pending(&self, name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.raw.time_audience_pending(name, id) }
    pub(crate) fn time_audience_published(&self, name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.raw.time_audience_published(name, id) }
    pub(crate) fn holds_assets(&self) -> bool { self.raw.holds_assets() }
    pub(crate) fn widen_change_evidence(&self) { self.raw.widen_change_evidence() }
    /// Draw a schema-fixed number of rows from the request's physical
    /// allowance up front, so later authorization work exhausts it at a point
    /// independent of the rows a delete's actions reach (LLP 3000.039 §4.2).
    pub(crate) fn draw(&self, table: &str, rows: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let rows = usize::try_from(rows).unwrap_or(usize::MAX);
        if self.remaining.get() < rows { self.remaining.set(0); return Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Delete }); }
        self.remaining.set(self.remaining.get() - rows);
        Ok(())
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.039#5.1 — reserved action hooks spend the physical draw once; ordinary root probes remain outside this scope.
    pub(super) fn reference_prepaid(&self, prepaid: bool) -> bool { self.reference_prepaid.replace(prepaid) }
    pub(crate) fn asset(&self, id: &str) -> Result<Option<Asset>, RuntimeError> { self.raw.asset(id) }
    pub(crate) fn claim_asset(&self, id: &str, by: &str, table: &str, row: &str, column: &str) -> Result<Asset, RuntimeError> { self.raw.claim_asset(id, by, table, row, column) }
    pub(crate) fn release_asset(&self, id: &str, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] table: &str, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] row_id: &str, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] column: &str, now: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> { #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] { self.raw.release_asset(id, table, row_id, column, now) } #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))] { self.raw.release_asset(id, now) } }
    pub(crate) fn effect_once(&self, effect: &str, key: &str) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.effect_once(effect, key) }
    pub(crate) fn provisional_id_after(&self, table: &str, after: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> { self.raw.provisional_id_after(table, after) }
    pub(crate) fn has_provisional(&self, table: &str) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.has_provisional(table) }
    pub(crate) fn provisional_groups(&self, table: &str, fields: &[String]) -> Result<Option<Vec<Vec<crate::value::Value>>>, RuntimeError> { self.raw.provisional_groups(table, fields) }
    pub(crate) fn provisional_row(&self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.provisional_row(table, row) }
    pub(crate) fn search_retained_row(&self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.search_retained_row(table, row) }
    pub(crate) fn retained_row(&self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.retained_row(table, row) }
    pub(crate) fn retained_group(&self, table: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.retained_group(table, index, prefix) }
    /// Device acquisition facts carry no row payload, like retained_row.
    pub(crate) fn current_row(&self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.current_row(table, row) }
    pub(crate) fn current_group(&self, table: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.raw.current_group(table, index, prefix) }
    pub(crate) fn recording_covered(&self, table: &str, row: &Value, viewer: &str, now: i64, scope: &Authorization, meter: &mut Meter) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { InternalLedger::with_meter(meter, |meter, _| {

    pub(crate) fn acquired_boundary(&self, table: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value]) -> Result<Option<AcquiredBoundary>, RuntimeError> { self.raw.acquired_boundary(table, index, prefix) }
    pub(crate) fn prefetch_enabled(&self) -> bool { self.raw.prefetch_enabled() }
    #[allow(dead_code)] // Used by the native prefetch planner; absent on web.
    pub(crate) fn prefetch(&self, requests: &[PrefetchRequest]) { self.raw.prefetch(requests) }
}

    fn fact_text(value: &Expr) -> String {
        let mut value = analysis_expr(value);
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut value, &mut |expr| {
            if is_viewer(expr) { *expr = Expr::Name { name: "viewer".into(), site: expr.site().clone() }; }
        });
        crate::print::expression(&value)
    }
    fn facts(condition: &Expr, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = condition && op == "and" { facts(left, out); facts(right, out); }
        else { out.insert(fact_text(condition)); }
    }
    fn readable(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, read: &ReadExpr, known: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
        let Some(table) = schema.table(&read.table) else { return true };
        if super::owner_equality::readable(catalog, schema, read, known) { return true; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable(); };
        let mut pinned = BTreeMap::new();
        // Unlike older advisory lints, proof cannot guess a non-id key: typed ids
        // may select byId even beside one same-width principal/composite key.
        let selected = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|index| &index.name == name));
        let inferred: Vec<_> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len()).collect();
        let positional_index = if read.index.is_some() { selected.filter(|index| index.is_plain()) }
            else { match inferred.as_slice() { [index] => Some(*index), _ => None } };
        if let Some(index) = positional_index {
            for (column, key) in index.columns.iter().zip(&read.keys) {
                pinned.insert(key.column.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| column.clone()), key.value.clone());
            }
        }
        // Extra named tests pin fields even when the positional prefix is unresolved.
        if selected.is_none_or(|index| index.computed.is_empty()) {
            for part in &read.keys { if let Some(column) = &part.column { pinned.insert(column.clone(), part.value.clone()); } }
        }
        if read.index.is_none() && read.keys.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_none()).count() == 1 && read.keys.first().is_some_and(|part| part.column.is_none() && is_viewer(&part.value)) {
            let principal_keys: Vec<_> = table.indexes.values().filter(|index| index.unique && index.columns().len() == 1
                && table.columns.get(&index.columns()[0]) == Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)).collect();
            if let [index] = principal_keys.as_slice() { pinned.insert(index.columns()[0].clone(), read.keys[0].value.clone()); }
        }
        let mut filters = Vec::new();
        if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { super::intent::split((**filter).clone(), &mut filters); }
        for filter in filters {
            if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = &filter && op == "="
                && let Some(column) = super::intent::column(left) { pinned.entry(column.to_owned()).or_insert_with(|| (**right).clone()); }
        }
        // A total self-membership key is witnessed by the probed row itself.

            target == &read.table && equalities.is_empty() && table.index(index).is_some_and(|index|
                index.unique && index.columns().len() == key.len() && index.columns().iter().zip(key).all(|(column, value)| {
                    let required = column == "id" || table.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|kind| !matches!(kind, snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Optional(_)));
                    required && (matches!(value, RuleTerm::Field(field) if field == column)
                        || matches!(value, RuleTerm::Viewer) && pinned.get(column).is_some_and(is_viewer))
                }))
        }) { return true; }
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm| -> Option<String> {
            match term {
                RuleTerm::Viewer => Some("viewer".into()),
                RuleTerm::Literal(value) => Some(RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(value.clone())).quarry().trim_start_matches("viewer = ").to_owned()),
                RuleTerm::Field(column) => pinned.get(column).map(fact_text),
                _ => None,
            }
        };
        fn holds(rule: &RuleExpr, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> Option<String>, schema: &Schema, known: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
            match rule {
                RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => true,
                RuleExpr::Eq(a, b) => match (term(a), term(b)) {
                    (Some(a), Some(b)) => a == b || known.contains(&format!("({a} = {b})")) || known.contains(&format!("({b} = {a})")),
                    _ => false,
                },
                RuleExpr::And(items) => items.iter().all(|rule| holds(rule, term, schema, known)),
                RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|rule| holds(rule, term, schema, known)),
                RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } => {
                    let Some(target) = schema.table(table).and_then(|t| t.index(index)) else { return false };
                    let Some(key) = key.iter().map(term).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>() else { return false };
                    let plain = format!("exists {table}[{}]", key.join(", "));
                    let Some(extra) = equalities.iter().map(|(c, t)| term(t).map(|v| format!("{c} = {v}"))).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>() else { return false };
                    let named = target.columns().iter().zip(&key).map(|(c, v)| format!("{c} = {v}"))
                        .chain(extra).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                    // Positional spellings with multiple compatible index widths
                    // are not evidence for this particular raw-rule key.
                    let source = schema.table(table).unwrap();
                    let unambiguous = source.indexes.values().filter(|candidate| candidate.unique && candidate.columns().len() == key.len()).count() == 1

                            && source.columns.get(&target.columns()[0]) == Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)
                            && source.indexes.values().filter(|candidate| candidate.unique && candidate.columns().len() == 1
                                && source.columns.get(&candidate.columns()[0]) == Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)).count() == 1;
                    (equalities.is_empty() && unambiguous && known.contains(&plain)) || known.contains(&format!("exists {table}[{named}]"))
                },
                _ => false,
            }
        }
        holds(&table.rules.read, &term, schema, known)
    }
    fn guard(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, condition: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        if system { return; }
        let mut reads = Vec::new();
        }
    }
    // Reverse dependency closure identifies query values consumed by guards,
    // across aliases/objects and all possible rebinding paths. The observation
    // is checked at its original statement, with that statement's authority.
    fn has_guard(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt], visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
        if statements.iter().any(|stmt| matches!(stmt, Stmt::Require { .. } | Stmt::If { .. })) { return true; }
        let mut copy = analysis_body(statements); let mut found = false;
        super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
            if matches!(expr, Expr::If { .. }) { found = true; }
            for (name, shape) in definitions(catalog, expr) {
                let key = format!("{shape}:{name}");
                if !visiting.insert(key.clone()) { found = true; continue; }
                found |= helper_body(catalog, &name, shape).is_some_and(|body| has_guard(catalog, &body, visiting));
                visiting.remove(&key);
            }
        });
        // Explicit guards can also be nested in loops/viewer scopes.
        found || statements.iter().any(|stmt| match stmt { Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => has_guard(catalog, body, visiting), _ => false })
    }
    fn guarded_inputs(catalog: &Catalog, expr: &Expr) -> Vec<Expr> {
        let consumed = definitions(catalog, expr).iter().any(|(name, shape)| helper_body(catalog, name, *shape)
            .is_some_and(|body| has_guard(catalog, &body, &mut BTreeSet::from([format!("{shape}:{name}")]))));
        match expr {
            Expr::Call { args, .. } if consumed => args.clone(),
            Expr::ShapeUse { base, .. } if consumed => vec![(**base).clone()],
            Expr::Shape { base, fields, .. } => {
                let values: Vec<_> = fields.iter().filter_map(|field| if let ShapeField::Field { value, site, .. } = field { Some(Stmt::Expr { value: value.clone(), site: site.clone() }) } else { None }).collect();
                if consumed || has_guard(catalog, &values, &mut BTreeSet::new()) { vec![(**base).clone()] } else { vec![] }
            },
            _ => vec![],
        }
    }
    fn guard_bindings(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt]) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        fn add_names(expr: &Expr, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
            if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr { out.insert(name.clone()); }
            if let Expr::Object { fields, .. } | Expr::Write { fields, .. } = expr {
                out.extend(fields.iter().filter(|field| field.value.is_none() && !field.spread).map(|field| field.name.clone()));
            }
        }
        fn names(value: &Expr) -> BTreeSet<String> {
            let mut out = BTreeSet::new(); let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| add_names(expr, &mut out)); out
        }
        fn input_names(value: &Expr) -> BTreeSet<String> {
            let mut out = BTreeSet::new(); value_nodes(value, &mut |expr| add_names(expr, &mut out)); out
        }
        fn gather(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt], guards: &mut BTreeSet<String>, edges: &mut BTreeMap<String, BTreeSet<String>>) {
            let mut copy = analysis_body(statements);
            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
                if let Expr::If { condition, .. } = expr { guards.extend(names(condition)); }
                // A definition can consume its input as a guard even when the
                // call itself is not a conditional. Conservatively track all
                // argument/base dependencies of such definitions.
                for input in guarded_inputs(catalog, expr) { guards.extend(input_names(&input)); }
            });
            for statement in statements {
                match statement {
                    Stmt::Bind { names: bindings, value, .. } => for name in bindings { edges.entry(name.clone()).or_default().extend(names(value)); },
                    Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => guards.extend(names(condition)),

                    Stmt::For { name, domain, body, .. } => { edges.entry(name.clone()).or_default().extend(names(domain)); gather(catalog, body, guards, edges); }

            guards.extend(next); if count == guards.len() { return guards; }
        }
    }

        }
    }

    pub(super) fn authorized_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.reserved_scratch(bytes, allowance, true, work)
    }

    /// Count certification follows an admitted member and source-group verdict.
    /// Nested authorization still shares any enclosing physical allowance.
    pub(super) fn disclosed_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.reserved_scratch(bytes, allowance, false, work)
    }

    pub(super) fn reserved_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], physical: bool, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device_work_active || self.context.is_system() { return self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work); }
        let mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
        let result = self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work);
        self.meter.release_row_heap(mark, bytes);
        result
    }

    pub(super) fn rule_scratch(&self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> u64 {
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        super::rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        sites.iter().map(|(table, _)| self.witness_scratch(table)).max().unwrap_or(0)
    }

    pub(super) fn witness_scratch(&self, table: &str) -> u64 {
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let own = crate::rows::disclosed::admission(definition);
        let source = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            .and_then(|m| self.schema.table(&m.source)).filter(|source| source.is_expiring());
        own.saturating_add(source.map_or(0, crate::rows::disclosed::admission))
    }


    /// Ordinary data reads keep their existing meter and cut semantics. This
    /// scope grants storage access without disabling or restoring any charge.
    /// Hidden authorization must use authorized_scan's prepaid reservation.
    pub(crate) fn metered_scan<T>(&mut self, scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        scan(self, &Authorization::internal())
    }

    /// A rule whose logical allowance the caller has already charged, possibly
    /// to a cut, still evaluates its witnesses under the physical allowance;
    /// the caller decides what its cut means for the answer (LLP 3000.014 §5).
    pub(super) fn prepaid_witnesses<T>(&mut self, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        let active = self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || !self.context.is_system());
        let result = work(self, &Authorization::internal());
        self.tx.authorizing.set(active);
        result
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — admission depends on declared bounds, never on
    // occupants. Private scratch cannot cut the caller's prepaid scan.
    pub(crate) fn authorized_scan<T: Default>(&mut self, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.prepaid_scan(allowance, true, scan)
    }

    /// Public or already-authorized data reads still reserve their logical
    /// meter; they do no authorization traversal. A nested scope cannot opt
    /// out of its enclosing authorization's physical allowance.
    pub(super) fn prepaid_scan<T: Default>(&mut self, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], physical: bool, scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        // This meter is the device's separate physical-work budget. Its
        // caller maps exhaustion to uncertainty, never a logical E_BOUND.
        if self.device_work_active {
            let active = self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || (physical && !self.context.is_system()));
            let result = scan(self, &Authorization::internal());
            self.tx.authorizing.set(active);
            return result;
        }
        for &(kind, amount) in allowance {
            if !self.read_charge(kind, amount)? { return Ok(T::default()); }
        }
        if self.read_capped() { return Ok(T::default()); }
        let scope = Authorization { probes: Cell::new(0), remaining_probes: self.meter.ceilings.rule_probes.saturating_sub(self.meter.used.rule_probes) };
        let state = self.enter_prepaid(physical);
        let result = scan(self, &scope);
        self.leave_prepaid(state, result.as_ref().err());
        if scope.probes.get() > 0 { self.read_charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, scope.probes.get())?; }
        result
    }

    // Kept out of the generic closure wrapper: every protected access shares
    // the same scope transition instead of instantiating its restoration code.
    #[inline(never)]
    pub(super) fn enter_prepaid(&mut self, physical: bool) -> PrepaidState {
        let nested = self.caller_heap_ceiling.is_some();
        self.caller_heap_ceiling.get_or_insert((self.meter.ceilings.heap_bytes, self.meter.used.heap_bytes));
        PrepaidState {
            mark: self.meter.mark(),
            ceilings: std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter.ceilings, Ceilings::unbounded()),
            cut: std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter.cut, Cut::Abort),
            authorizing: self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || (physical && !self.context.is_system())),
            nested,
        }
    }

    #[inline(never)]
    pub(super) fn leave_prepaid(&mut self, state: PrepaidState, error: Option<&RuntimeError>) {
        self.tx.authorizing.set(state.authorizing);
        self.meter.restore(state.mark);
        self.meter.ceilings = state.ceilings;
        self.meter.cut = state.cut;
        if !state.nested { self.caller_heap_ceiling = None; }
        if let Some(RuntimeError::Bound { kind: MeterKind::HeapBytes, site, .. }) = error {
            self.meter.capped_at.get_or_insert((MeterKind::HeapBytes, site.clone()));
        }
    }

}

mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{Context, Store, meter::Meter, schema::Schema};

    #[test]
    fn job_authority_edges_clear_raw_cache_but_keep_the_allowance() {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let schema = Schema::default();
        let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &schema, Context::system(), Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort), 0);
        runner.tx.remaining.set(17);
        *runner.tx.lookup_cache.borrow_mut() = Some(("t".into(), "byId".into(), vec![], None));
        let child = runner.job_read_child(Context::new("user:alice", Default::default()));
        assert!(child.tx.lookup_cache.borrow().is_none());
        assert_eq!(child.tx.remaining(), 17);
        child.tx.remaining.set(13);
        *child.tx.lookup_cache.borrow_mut() = Some(("t".into(), "byId".into(), vec![], None));
        runner.finish_job_read_child(child);
        assert!(runner.tx.lookup_cache.borrow().is_none());
        assert_eq!(runner.tx.remaining(), 13);
    }

            ("secret".into(),ColumnType::Text { max: 1000000 })].into(), Default::default());
        table.indexes.insert("byId".into(),crate::schema::Index::of(&["id"],true));
        table.written_by.insert("secret".into(),FieldAuthority { owner:"owner".into(),audience:false });
        for (name, table) in &self.schema.tables {
            if table.sequences.contains_key(field) && self.is_provisional(name, row)? { return Ok(true); }

    pub(super) fn evaluate_sequence_rule(&mut self, allowance: &Authorization, name: &str, rule: &RuleExpr, effect: Effect, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let next_provisional = self.device && (effect == Effect::Insert || self.is_provisional(name, next)?);
        let old_provisional = old.map(|row| self.is_provisional(name, row)).transpose()?.unwrap_or(false);
        let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
        super::rules::evaluate(rule, &viewer, old, next, effect == Effect::Insert, &mut SequenceProbe {
            runner: self, allowance, table: name, old: old_provisional, next: next_provisional,
        })
    }

    pub(super) fn observe_sequence_columns(&mut self, name: &str, row: &Value, fields: &[String], inserting: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && fields.iter().any(|field| self.schema.tables[name].sequences.contains_key(field)) {
            self.provisional_sequence_read |= inserting || self.is_provisional(name, row)?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Author-facing point reads and pages share the sequence result contract.
    pub(super) fn sequence_read_result(&mut self, name: &str, mut row: Value) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        if row != Value::Null && row.field("pending") != Ok(&Value::Bool(true)) && (!self.schema.tables[name].sequences.is_empty() || self.schema.receipt_insert(name)) && self.is_provisional(name, &row)? {
            let bytes = self.project_row(name, row.clone())?.heap_bytes();
            self.meter.heap(bytes + word_heap::insertion::<String, Value>(row.as_object()?.len()) + word_heap::text("pending"))?;
            if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row { fields.insert("pending".into(), Value::Bool(true)); }
        }
        Ok(row)
    }

# Rules — E_RULE

`insert` and `insert ... if absent` return E_RULE when the insert rule denies
the viewer. A unique clash with a hidden row returns E_RULE for either insert
form. For a readable secondary unique clash without a custom refusal, ordinary
insert returns E_UNIQUE naming the declared key, while `insert ... if absent`
succeeds with null. If the update rule denies an update or upsert-patch, it
returns E_RULE for a readable target and NOT_FOUND for an unreadable target. A
valid update or upsert-patch admitted by the update rule can change a hidden
row; if the resulting row remains hidden, it returns null. Without a custom
unique refusal, an update or upsert-patch with a secondary unique clash returns
NOT_FOUND if its target is unreadable, E_RULE if only the colliding row is
unreadable, and E_UNIQUE naming the declared key if both are readable. For rule
denial, an update condition or a secondary unique clash on an unreadable target,
NOT_FOUND carries no row, index, column or branch detail. A missing update
target or an incomplete upsert insert patch returns NOT_FOUND. An update
condition on an unreadable target returns NOT_FOUND even if it would match; on a
readable target, a false condition returns E_CONFLICT with a read-rule
`current`. Delete returns NOT_FOUND for a missing id or an unreadable refused target, and E_RULE for a readable row whose delete rule refuses. Reading a
hidden row returns null. Use a write admitted by its rule; NOT_FOUND on an
update or upsert-patch does not prove absence.

A read denied by a table's rule answers as an empty page, not a refusal,
and a client cannot tell it apart from an empty readable page — by design,
so that a hidden row cannot be probed. For access UI, use a membership query
the viewer may read, such as a conversation lookup readable by its members:
a member gets the conversation even when its message page is empty.
An explicit `require … else CODE` still refuses with the program's code.

A mutation's writes pass the table's rules as the caller; only jobs run as the
system. Under `dev`, `snapback4 why recent` prints each refusal and parked job:
operation, code, request/job ID, Unix time, and the refusing clause with its
site. Replays and uninstrumented paths say `cause not instrumented`. Records describe only
the named request/job.
Visibility filtering is not recorded. Inspect `snapback4 why TABLE` for rules
and residency. Unknown empty answers are silent and unrecorded by default;
`dev --trace-empty` records them locally without console notices.
`snapback4 why EMPTY_READ` gives rule, coverage and horizon investigation commands.

Console repeats print counted one-second windows; `guide refusals` explains.

With several live instances select `why recent --instance ID` (optionally
`--store ID`). Owner-only `.snapback4/dev-refusals/<storeId>/<instance>.json`
retains 32 records. Stopped/mismatched streams are rejected. The asynchronous
32-slot queue reports `dropped` on recovery; `why recent` refuses older
snapshots while newer refusal evidence is pending or dropped.
Query sites belong to the reader's generation; capture exhaustion points to
`read through`. No arguments or row values are recorded, no HTTP endpoint
exposes evidence, and production `serve` does not collect it. Caller bytes
remain unchanged.

    pub fn replay_mutation(&self, viewer: &str, id: &str, name: &str, args: &serde_json::Value) -> Result<Option<MutationOutcome>, RuntimeError> {
        crate::store::replay_bound(self.store.conn(), viewer, id, name, args)?
            .map(|receipt| MutationOutcome::from_receipt(&receipt)).transpose()
    }

    /// Run a mutation. With an `op` id the write applies at most once: a
    /// replay returns the recorded outcome. The replay check comes before
    /// anything the current generation could refuse (an op renamed, an
    /// argument narrowed), so a write that applied under an earlier schema
    /// is never re-judged: its row is on the server, and the device that
    /// queued it must learn `sent`, not `failed`.
    pub fn mutate(&mut self, name: &str, op: Option<&str>, mut context: Context) -> Result<MutationOutcome, RuntimeError> {
        context.generation = self.generation;
        let requested = context.now.unwrap_or_else(crate::now_ms);
        context.now = Some(self.advance_time_audience(requested)?);
        let wire_args = if op.is_some() { context.wire_args.take().unwrap_or_else(|| {
                serde_json::Value::Object(crate::wire::map_to_json(&context.args))
            }) } else { serde_json::Value::Null };
        if let Some(op) = op
            && let Some(outcome) = self.replay_mutation(&context.viewer, op, name, &wire_args)?
        {
            return Ok(outcome);
        }
        // An existing durable receipt owns its id even after conversion to ephemeral.
        if self.schema.operations.get(name).is_some_and(|f| f.ephemeral) {
            self.require_non_media_ephemeral(&context.viewer,op)?;
            self.schema.check_delivery(name, context.delivery_fingerprint.as_deref())?;
            let viewer=context.viewer.clone();
            return match self.mutate_ephemeral(name, context) {
                Ok(out) => Ok(MutationOutcome { receipt_only: false, state: WriteState::Sent { seq: out.seq, result: out.result }, metered: out.metered, tables: out.ephemeral.tables, events: 0, replayed: false }),
                Err(error) if error.is_refusal() => Ok(MutationOutcome { receipt_only: false, state: WriteState::Failed { why: seat_refusal(&self.schema,&error,&viewer), current: None }, metered: Metered::default(), tables: Vec::new(), events: 0, replayed: false }),
                Err(error) => Err(error),
            };
        }
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        if let Some(op) = op
            && let Some(outcome) = crate::store::replay_bound(tx.conn(), &context.viewer, op, name, &wire_args)?
        {
            // Applied between the check above and this transaction.
            let outcome = MutationOutcome::from_receipt(&outcome)?;
            tx.rollback()?;
            return Ok(outcome);
        }
        if let Some(op)=op{assets::require_placement(&tx,&context.viewer,op,name,&wire_args)?;}
        context.now = Some(time_audience::pin_writer_time(
            &self.schema,
            &tx,
            context.now.unwrap_or(0),
        )?);
        self.schema.check_delivery(name, context.delivery_fingerprint.as_deref())?;
        let program = self.programs.get(name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::UnknownOp(name.to_owned()))?.clone();
        if program.kind == ProgramKind::Query {
            return Err(RuntimeError::UnknownOp(format!("{name} is a query, not a mutation")));
        }
        if program.kind == ProgramKind::Action {
            return Err(RuntimeError::UnknownOp(format!("{name} is an action: call it with client.act")));
        }
        if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job {
            return Err(RuntimeError::UnknownOp(format!("{name} is a job: a schedule fires it, or `snapback4 run {name}` does; no caller can")));
        }
        if let Err(error) = validate::validate_inputs(&self.schema, &program, &mut context.args) {
            return Ok(MutationOutcome { receipt_only: false, state: WriteState::Failed { why: error.refusal(), current: None }, metered: Metered::default(), tables: Vec::new(), events: 0, replayed: false });
        }
        if context.presence.is_none() {
            context.presence = self.presence.clone();
        }
        context.entropy = self.entropy;
        let ceilings = self.ceilings;
        let viewer = context.viewer.clone();
        let now = context.now.unwrap_or(0);
        // An `image`/`video` argument names an upload by id; the program
        // reads the facts the server established (`clip.duration`), so
        // they are put in place before it runs. An id that is not the
        // writer's own upload stays as sent and the write refuses it.
        for (name, kind) in &program.args {
            let inner = match kind {
                crate::ir::ArgType::Optional(inner) => inner.as_ref(),
                other => other,
            };
            if !matches!(inner, crate::ir::ArgType::Image { .. } | crate::ir::ArgType::Video { .. } | crate::ir::ArgType::File { .. }) {
                continue;
            }
            let Some(id) = asset_id(context.args.get(name)).map(str::to_owned) else { continue;
            };
            if let Some(asset) = tx.asset(&id)?
                && (asset.owner == viewer || context.is_system())
            {
                // @ref LLP 3000.038#3 — a file argument never reads an image's facts, nor an image argument a file's.
                if (asset.kind == AssetKind::File) != matches!(inner, crate::ir::ArgType::File { .. }) {
                    let wanted = if matches!(inner, crate::ir::ArgType::File { .. }) { "a file" } else { "an image or video" };
                    let error = RuntimeError::Asset { code: "E_ASSET_TYPE", message: format!("{name} takes {wanted} and {id} is {}; upload it as {wanted}", asset.kind.word()) };
                    return Ok(MutationOutcome { receipt_only: false, state: WriteState::Failed { why: error.refusal(), current: None }, metered: Metered::default(), tables: Vec::new(), events: 0, replayed: false });
                }
                context.args.insert(name.clone(), asset.column_value());
            }
        }
        tx.savepoint("program")?;
        let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &self.schema, context, Meter::new(ceilings, Cut::Abort), tx.next_seq()?);
        let ran = runner.run(&program).and_then(|value| {
            runner.check_final_exists()?;
            runner.charge_output(&value)?;
            Ok(value)
        });
        let metered = runner.meter.used;
        let ran = ran.and_then(|value| runner.retire_expired_births().map(|_| value));
        let tables: Vec<String> = runner.written_tables.iter().cloned().collect();
        let events: Vec<Event> = runner.events.take();
        let action_tables = std::mem::take(&mut runner.action_tables);
        let outbox = std::mem::take(&mut runner.outbox);
        let rows_written = runner.rows_written;
        let mut read_tables = runner.read_tables.clone();
        self.entropy = runner.context.entropy;
        drop(runner);
        let outcome = match ran {
            Ok(result) => {
                tx.release("program")?;
                // @ref LLP 3000.019#4 — P7 reports keep at-most-once custody.
                // @ref LLP 3000.022#6 — a covered report keeps only a receipt at the committed head.
                let receipt_only = rows_written == 0 && outbox.is_empty()
                    && self.schema.delivery.iter().any(|binding| binding.mutation == name);
                let seq = tx.next_seq()? - u64::from(receipt_only);
                read_tables.extend(tables.iter().cloned());
                if !receipt_only {
                    tx.record_commit(seq, now, name, &viewer, program.kind.word(), &tables, rows_written)?;
                    partition::append(&tx, seq, &events)?;
                    effects::record(&tx, &outbox, seq, now, self.dev_effects, &self.schema)?;
                }
                MutationOutcome { receipt_only, state: WriteState::Sent { seq, result }, metered, tables, events: partition::disclosed_event_count(&self.schema, &events, &action_tables), replayed: false }
            }
            Err(error) if error.is_refusal() => {
                tx.rollback_to("program")?;
                let current = match &error {
                    RuntimeError::Conflict { current } => {
                        let mut probe = Runner::new(&tx, &self.schema, Context::new(viewer.clone(), BTreeMap::new()).at(now), Meter::new(Ceilings::default(), Cut::Cap), 0);
                        let value = probe.get(current).ok();
                        value
                    }
                    _ => None,
                };
                let why=seat_refusal(&self.schema,&error,&viewer);
                MutationOutcome { receipt_only: false, state: WriteState::Failed { why, current }, metered, tables: Vec::new(), events: 0, replayed: false }
            }
            Err(error) => {
                tx.rollback()?;
                return Err(error);
            }
        };
