//! The binder's type model: what a Quarry expression is, statically, so a
//! program's arguments and result reach the client as TypeScript types by
//! inference (nothing is annotated, nothing is committed).

use crate::ast::{ScalarType, TypeKind};
use snapback4_core::ir::ArgType;
use snapback4_core::schema::{ColumnType, TextFormat};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Ty {
    Unknown,
    Null,
    Bool,
    Int,
    Time,
    Decimal(u8),
    Money(String, u8),
    Text(u32),
    Id,
    Principal,
    Ref(String),
    Bytes,
    Json,
    Image,
    Video,
    File,
    Enum(Vec<String>),
    Cursor,
    Optional(Box<Ty>),
    /// A bounded list of scalars; `max` bounds a loop over it.
    List(Box<Ty>, u32),
    /// One row of a table, present.
    Row(String),
    WriteRow(String),
    /// A bounded page of rows, in index order.
    Page(String, u32),
    /// A shaped page: at most `max` objects.
    Collection(Box<Ty>, u32),
    Object(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),
    /// A projection whose row base carries optional device metadata.
    RowShape(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),
    /// Object-spread fields with row metadata, but ordinary object wildcard order.
    RowObject(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),

}

impl Ty {
    /// A reassigned allocation is bounded by the heap meter, not its first literal's length.
    pub(crate) fn rebound(self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Text(_) => Ty::Text(u32::MAX),
            // A known empty value stays empty, even at a loop header.
            Ty::List(_, 0) | Ty::Collection(_, 0) => self,
            Ty::List(item, _) => Ty::List(Box::new(item.rebound()), u32::MAX),
            Ty::Collection(item, _) => Ty::Collection(Box::new(item.rebound()), u32::MAX),
            Ty::Object(fields) => Ty::Object(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::RowShape(fields) => Ty::RowShape(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::RowObject(fields) => Ty::RowObject(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::Optional(inner) => inner.rebound().optional(),
            other => other,
        }
    }

    pub fn optional(self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Optional(_) | Ty::Null | Ty::Unknown => self,
            other => Ty::Optional(Box::new(other)),
        }
    }

    pub fn required(&self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Optional(inner) => inner.as_ref().clone(),
            other => other.clone(),
        }
    }

    pub fn is_optional(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Optional(_) | Ty::Null)
    }

    pub fn is_bool(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Bool)
    }

    pub fn is_numeric(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Int | Ty::Time | Ty::Decimal(_) | Ty::Money(..))
    }

    pub fn is_identity(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Id | Ty::Principal | Ty::Ref(_))
    }

    pub fn from_scalar(scalar: &ScalarType) -> Ty {
        let base = match &scalar.kind {
            TypeKind::Cursor => Ty::Cursor,
            TypeKind::Text { max, .. } => Ty::Text(*max),
            TypeKind::Id => Ty::Id,
            TypeKind::Principal => Ty::Principal,
            TypeKind::Int | TypeKind::IntRange { .. } => Ty::Int,
            TypeKind::Decimal { scale } => Ty::Decimal(*scale),
            TypeKind::Money { currency, scale } => Ty::Money(currency.clone(), *scale),
            TypeKind::Time => Ty::Time,
            TypeKind::Bool => Ty::Bool,
            TypeKind::Json { .. } => Ty::Json,
            TypeKind::Bytes { .. } => Ty::Bytes,
            TypeKind::Image { .. } => Ty::Image,
            TypeKind::Video { .. } => Ty::Video,
            TypeKind::File { .. } => Ty::File,
            TypeKind::Enum { variants } => Ty::Enum(variants.clone()),
            TypeKind::Ref { table } => Ty::Ref(table.clone()),
            TypeKind::List { item, max, .. } => Ty::List(Box::new(Ty::from_scalar(item)), *max),
        };
        if scalar.optional { base.optional() } else { base }
    }

    pub fn from_column(kind: &ColumnType) -> Ty {
        match kind {
            ColumnType::Id => Ty::Id,
            ColumnType::Principal => Ty::Principal,
            ColumnType::Text { max } => Ty::Text(*max),
            ColumnType::Bytes { .. } => Ty::Bytes,
            ColumnType::Image { .. } => Ty::Image,
            ColumnType::Video { .. } => Ty::Video,
            ColumnType::File { .. } => Ty::File,
            ColumnType::Int | ColumnType::IntRange { .. } => Ty::Int,
            ColumnType::Enum { variants } => Ty::Enum(variants.clone()),
            ColumnType::Decimal { scale } => Ty::Decimal(*scale),
            ColumnType::Money { currency, scale } => Ty::Money(currency.clone(), *scale),
            ColumnType::Bool => Ty::Bool,
            ColumnType::Time => Ty::Time,
            ColumnType::Ref { table } => Ty::Ref(table.clone()),
            ColumnType::Json { .. } => Ty::Json,
            ColumnType::Optional(inner) => Ty::from_column(inner).optional(),
            ColumnType::List { of, max } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, max, .. } => Ty::List(Box::new(Ty::from_column(of)), *max),
        }
    }

    /// The name a diagnostic prints.
    pub fn name(&self) -> String {
        match self {
            Ty::Unknown => "an unknown value".into(),
            Ty::Null => "null".into(),
            Ty::Bool => "bool".into(),
            Ty::Int => "int".into(),
            Ty::Time => "time".into(),
            Ty::Decimal(scale) => format!("decimal {scale}"),
            Ty::Money(currency, scale) => format!("money {currency} {scale}"),
            Ty::Text(max) => format!("text <={max}"),
            Ty::Id => "id".into(),
            Ty::Principal => "principal".into(),
            Ty::Ref(table) => format!("a {table} reference"),
            Ty::Bytes => "bytes".into(),
            Ty::Json => "json".into(),
            Ty::Image => "image".into(),
            Ty::Video => "video".into(),
            Ty::File => "file".into(),
            Ty::Enum(variants) => format!("enum({})", variants.iter().map(|v| format!("'{v}'")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
            Ty::Cursor => "cursor".into(),
            Ty::Optional(inner) => format!("{} ?", inner.name()),
            Ty::List(item, max) => format!("[{}] <={max}", item.name()),
            Ty::Row(table) => format!("a {table} row"),
            Ty::WriteRow(table) => format!("a {table} write result"),
            Ty::Page(table, max) => format!("a page of at most {max} {table} rows"),
            Ty::Collection(item, max) => format!("at most {max} of {}", item.name()),
            Ty::Object(fields) | Ty::RowShape(fields) | Ty::RowObject(fields) => format!("{{ {} }}", fields.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
        }
    }

    /// Whether a value of `self` may be stored where `expected` is declared.
    pub fn assignable_to(&self, expected: &Ty) -> bool {
        if self == expected {
            return true;
        }
        match (self, expected) {
            (Ty::Null, Ty::Optional(_)) => true,
            (Ty::Optional(inner), Ty::Optional(target)) => inner.assignable_to(target),
            (value, Ty::Optional(target)) => value.assignable_to(target),
            (Ty::Text(a), Ty::Text(b)) => a <= b,
            {
                out.extend(row_setup);
                let id = self.temp("key");
                out.push(Stmt::Let { name: id.clone(), value: ir::field(e, "id") });
                return Ok(WriteKey { index: "byId".into(), key: vec![ir::var(&id)], alternate: false });
            }
        }
        let read = ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: info.decl.name.clone(), keys: keys.to_vec(), index: None, range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), site: site.clone() };
        let (bound, index, _) = self.select_index(info, &read, ReadMode::Point, out)?;
        super::point_key::check(info, &index, &read, &bound.iter().map(|(column, _, ty)|
            (column.as_deref(), !ty.is_optional() && *ty != Ty::Unknown)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), false)?;
        let key = self.order_keys(info, &index, bound)?;
        if index.name == "byId" {
            let id = self.temp("key");
            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: id.clone(), value: key[0].clone() });
            return Ok(WriteKey { index: "byId".into(), key: vec![ir::var(&id)], alternate: false });
        }
        Ok(WriteKey { index: index.name, key, alternate: true })
    }

    fn write_row(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, fields: &[ObjectField], current: Option<&str>, site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        objects::explicit_fields(fields.iter().filter(|f| !f.spread).map(|f| (f.name.as_str(), &f.site)))?;
        let expanded;
        let fields = if fields.iter().any(|field| field.spread) {
            expanded = self.expand_object_fields(fields, Some(info), out)?;
            &expanded
        } else { fields };
        for field in fields {
            if super::columns::fields(info).any(|name|name == field.name) {
                return Err(super::columns::refusal(&field.site,format!("{}.{} is engine-maintained; write its source rows instead",info.decl.name,field.name)));
            }
        }
        match current {
            None => {
                if fields.iter().any(|f| f.name == "id") {
                    let (given, ty) = fields.iter().find(|f| f.name == "id").map(|f| f.value.as_ref().map(|v| self.expr_as_reference(v, out, &Ty::Id)).unwrap_or_else(|| self.name("id", site))).unwrap()?;
                    if !ty.assignable_to(&Ty::Id) { return Err(type_error(site,format!("an inserted id must be an id, got {}",ty.name()),"use row.id for a row snapshot; reference columns accept a required same-table row")); }
                    return self.write_row_with_id(info, &fields.iter().filter(|f| f.name != "id").cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>(), given, site, out);
                }
                self.write_row_with_id(info, fields, Expr::Input(Input::NewId), site, out)
            }
            Some(_) => {
                // A patch: only the named columns, each typed.
                let mut exprs = Vec::<(String, Expr)>::new();
                let mut types = BTreeMap::new();
                for field in fields {
                    if field.name == "id" {
                        return Err(unknown(&field.site, "update cannot change id", "remove id from the patch"));
                    }
                    let Some(scalar) = info.columns.get(&field.name) else {
                        return Err(unknown(&field.site, format!("{} has no column {}", info.decl.name, field.name), name_rewrite(&field.name, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str), None)));
                    };
                    let expected = Ty::from_scalar(scalar);
                    let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
                    let (e, t) = self.field_value(field, &expected, &mut setup)?;
                    literal_in_range(info, &field.name, scalar, &e, &field.site)?;
                    self.sequence_setup(exprs.iter_mut().map(|(_, e)| e), setup, out);
                    if !t.writable_to(&expected) {
                        return Err(diagnostics::write_field_type(info, field, &expected, &t));
                    }
                    let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
                    exprs.push((field.name.clone(), e));
                    if types.insert(field.name.clone(), t).is_some() {
                        return Err(unknown(&field.site, format!("field {} is written twice", field.name), "keep one"));
                    }
                }
                Ok((Expr::Object(exprs.into_iter().collect()), Ty::Object(types)))
            }
        }
    }

    fn write_row_with_id(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, fields: &[ObjectField], id: Expr, site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        let id = self.snapshot(id, out);
        let mut exprs = vec![("id".to_owned(), id)];
        let mut given = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
        for field in fields {
            let Some(scalar) = info.columns.get(&field.name) else {
                return Err(unknown(&field.site, format!("{} has no column {}", info.decl.name, field.name), name_rewrite(&field.name, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str), None)));
            };
            let expected = Ty::from_scalar(scalar);
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let (e, t) = self.field_value(field, &expected, &mut setup)?;
            literal_in_range(info, &field.name, scalar, &e, &field.site)?;
            self.sequence_setup(exprs.iter_mut().map(|(_, e)| e), setup, out);
            if !t.writable_to(&expected) {
                return Err(diagnostics::write_field_type(info, field, &expected, &t));
            }
            let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
            if !given.insert(field.name.clone()) {
                return Err(unknown(&field.site, format!("field {} is written twice", field.name), "keep one"));
            }
            exprs.push((field.name.clone(), e));
        }
        // Materialize the same defaults in the insert expression so its returned row is the written image.
        for column in &info.decl.columns {
            if !given.contains(&column.name) && let Some(default) = &column.default {
                if column.written_by.is_some() || self.schema.receipt_assigned(&info.decl.name, &column.name) {
                    // Preserve omission until the engine checks supplied-field authority.
                    given.insert(column.name.clone());
                    continue;
                }
                let default = super::defaults::column(column, default)?;
                let value = match default {
                    snapback4_core::schema::ColumnDefault::Literal(value) => Expr::Literal(value),
                    snapback4_core::schema::ColumnDefault::Now => Expr::Input(Input::Now),
                    snapback4_core::schema::ColumnDefault::Viewer => Expr::Input(Input::Viewer),
                };
                exprs.push((column.name.clone(), value));
                given.insert(column.name.clone());
            }
        }
        let missing: Vec<&str> = info.columns.iter().filter(|(name, scalar)| !scalar.optional && !self.schema.receipt_assigned(&info.decl.name, name) && !info.decl.columns.iter().any(|c| c.name == **name && c.sequence.is_some()) && !super::columns::fields(info).any(|f|f == *name) && !given.contains(*name)).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).collect();
        if !missing.is_empty() {
            return Err(type_error(site, format!("insert into {} omits required column{} {}", info.decl.name, if missing.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, missing.join(", ")), format!("insert {} {{ {} }}", info.decl.name, given.iter().cloned().chain(missing.iter().map(|m| format!("{m}: <{}>", info.columns.get(*m).map(print::scalar_type).unwrap_or_default()))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))));
        }
        Ok((Expr::Object(exprs.into_iter().collect()), self.write_result_type(info)))
    }

    fn project_insert(&self, info: &TableInfo, row: Expr) -> Expr {
        if info.decl.columns.iter().any(|column| column.written_by.is_some()) {
            Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::ProjectFields { table: info.decl.name.clone() }, args: vec![row] }
        } else { row }
    }

    fn write_result_type(&self, info: &TableInfo) -> Ty {
        if super::columns::fields(info).next().is_some() { Ty::WriteRow(info.decl.name.clone()) } else { Ty::Row(info.decl.name.clone()) }
    }

    fn calendar_number(&self, value: &A, depth: u8) -> bool {
        if depth > 8 { return false; }
        match value {
            A::Call { name, .. } => matches!(name.as_str(), "day" | "hour" | "bucket"),
            A::Name { name, .. } => self.binding(name).is_some_and(|binding| binding.revision == 0 && binding.origins.len() == 1 && self.calendar_number(&binding.origins[0].1, depth + 1)),
            A::If { then, otherwise, .. } => self.calendar_number(then, depth + 1) || self.calendar_number(otherwise, depth + 1),
//! Bind key operands once, retaining authored sequencing and contextual literals.
use super::*;

impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
    pub(super) fn bind_key_parts(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, end: usize,
        keys: &mut Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
        for (position, part) in read.keys.iter().enumerate().take(end).skip(keys.len()) {
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let expected = part.column.as_deref().or_else(|| read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)).and_then(|i| i.columns.get(position)).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column));
            let (e, t) = match expected {
                Some(expected) => self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup, &expected)?,
                None => self.expr(&part.value, &mut setup)?,
            };
            self.sequence_setup(keys.iter_mut().map(|(_, e, _)| e), setup, out);
            let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
            keys.push((part.column.clone(), e, t));
        }
        if let Some(index) = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)) {
            for (position,(column,value,ty)) in keys.iter_mut().enumerate() {
                if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().or_else(|| index.columns.get(position).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|c| key_identity(info,c)) {
                    (*value,*ty) = self.row_identity(value.clone(),ty.clone(),&expected);
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    // Mixed-key normalization names an already-bound positional prefix. Only
    // a literal acquires new context; compound expressions keep their IR/setup.
    pub(super) fn name_bound_keys(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr,
        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {
        for ((column, expr, ty), part) in keys.iter_mut().zip(&read.keys) {
            *column = part.column.clone();
            if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column))
                && matches!(&part.value, A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(_), .. }) {
                (*expr, *ty) = self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup::Body::default(), &expected)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn key_identity(info: &TableInfo, column: &str) -> Option<Ty> {
    let ty = if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()) }
        else { Ty::from_scalar(info.columns.get(column)?) };
    ty.required().is_identity().then_some(ty)
}

//! A positional unique key followed by named tests is named-key sugar.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — only declared unique keys resolve positions; extra equalities test one row.
use super::{catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo}, intent_walk as walk};
use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, Site}, print};
use std::{borrow::Cow, collections::BTreeMap};

pub(super) fn keys<'a>(table: &TableInfo, parts: &'a [KeyPart], index: Option<&str>, site: &Site, synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>, types: Option<&[(crate::types::Ty, bool)]>) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, [KeyPart]>> {
    if !parts.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_some()) || !parts.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_none()) { return Ok(Cow::Borrowed(parts)); }
    let prefix = parts.iter().take_while(|key| key.column.is_none()).count();
    if let Some(late) = parts[prefix..].iter().find(|key| key.column.is_none()) {
        let ordered = parts.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_none()).chain(parts.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_some())).map(spelling).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        return Err(Diagnostic::new(&late.site, "E_QUARRY_KEY_ORDER", "positional key components come before named column tests",
            format!("{}[{ordered}]", table.decl.name)).guide("reads"));
    }
    let declared: Vec<_> = table.indexes.iter().filter(|key| key.unique
        && !synthesized.contains(&(table.decl.name.clone(), key.name.clone()))).collect();
    let candidates: Vec<_> = if let Some(types) = types {
        let read = ReadExpr { table: table.decl.name.clone(), keys: parts[..prefix].to_vec(), index: index.map(str::to_owned), site: site.clone(),
            cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new() };
        super::positional_keys::candidates(table, &read, types, synthesized).into_iter()
            .filter(|key| key.unique && key.columns.len() == prefix).collect()
    } else {
        // Before types are known, do not silently turn `roles[viewer,
        // role = 'staff']` into an id lookup merely because byId has the
        // positional prefix's arity. A declared composite key covered by the
        // positional prefix plus named suffix must reach the typed pass, which
        // prints the fully named repair.
        let named: Vec<&str> = parts[prefix..].iter().filter_map(|part| part.column.as_deref()).collect();
        let partial_composite = declared.iter().any(|key| key.columns.len() > prefix && key.columns.len() <= parts.len()
            && key.columns[prefix..].iter().all(|column| named.contains(&column.as_str()))
            && index.is_none_or(|name| name == key.name));
        if partial_composite { Vec::new() }
        else { declared.iter().copied().filter(|key| key.columns.len() == prefix && index.is_none_or(|name| name == key.name)).collect() }
    };
    let chosen = match candidates.as_slice() {
        [chosen] if table.total_unique(chosen) => *chosen,
        _ => {
            let suggested = candidates.first().copied().or_else(|| declared.iter().copied().find(|key| key.columns.len() > prefix)).or_else(|| declared.first().copied());
            let descriptions = declared.iter().map(|key| format!("({})", key.columns.join(", "))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" or ");
            let rewrite = if let Some(key) = suggested {
                let mut named = key.columns.iter().enumerate().map(|(at, column)| {
                    let value = parts.get(at).filter(|_| at < prefix).map(|part| print::expression(&part.value))
                        .or_else(|| parts.iter().find(|part| part.column.as_ref() == Some(column)).map(|part| print::expression(&part.value)))
                        .unwrap_or_else(|| format!("<{column}>"));
                    format!("{column} = {value}")
                }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                named.extend(parts[prefix..].iter().filter(|part| !key.columns.contains(part.column.as_ref().unwrap())).map(spelling));
                format!("{}[{}]", table.decl.name, named.join(", "))
            } else { format!("{}[id = <id>, {}]", table.decl.name, parts[prefix..].iter().map(spelling).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")) };
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN",
                format!("{} needs one full declared unique key with non-null columns before named tests; {prefix} positional components match {} keys; declared keys: {descriptions}", table.decl.name, candidates.len()), rewrite.clone())
                .source_rewrite(crate::diag::SourceRewriteKind::MixedKey, rewrite).guide("reads"));
        }
    };
    let mut named = parts.to_vec();
    for (part, column) in named.iter_mut().zip(&chosen.columns) { part.column = Some(column.clone()); }
    Ok(Cow::Owned(named))
}

fn spelling(part: &KeyPart) -> String {
    match &part.column { Some(column) => format!("{column} = {}", print::expression(&part.value)), None => print::expression(&part.value) }
}

pub(super) fn read<'a>(table: &TableInfo, read: &'a ReadExpr, catalog: &Catalog) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, ReadExpr>> {
    read_typed(table, read, catalog, None)
}

pub(super) fn read_typed<'a>(table: &TableInfo, read: &'a ReadExpr, catalog: &Catalog, types: Option<&[(crate::types::Ty, bool)]>) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, ReadExpr>> {
    Ok(match keys(table, &read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site, &catalog.synthesized, types)? {
        Cow::Borrowed(_) => Cow::Borrowed(read),
        Cow::Owned(keys) => {
            let index = types.and_then(|types| super::positional_keys::candidates(table, read, types, &catalog.synthesized)
                .first().map(|index| index.name.clone())).or_else(|| read.index.clone());
            Cow::Owned(ReadExpr { keys, index, ..read.clone() })
        },
    })
}

/// Normalize before planning and sync inference, including helpers and shapes.
/// Invalid keys remain for the ordinary binder to diagnose at their use site.
pub(super) fn normalize(catalog: &mut Catalog) {
    let tables: BTreeMap<_, _> = catalog.tables.clone();
    let mut visit = |value: &mut Expr| {
        let (name, parts, index, site) = match value {
            Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } | Expr::Created { read, .. } => (&read.table, &mut read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site),
            Expr::Write { table, keys, site, .. } => (&*table, keys, None, &*site),
            _ => return,
        };
        if let Some(table) = tables.get(name)
            && let Ok(Cow::Owned(named)) = keys(table, parts, index, site, &catalog.synthesized, None) { *parts = named; }
    };
    for table in catalog.tables.values_mut() {
        for rule in &mut table.decl.rules { walk::expression(&mut rule.value, &mut visit); }
        for (value, _) in &mut table.decl.final_exists { walk::expression(value, &mut visit); }
    }
    for doc in &mut catalog.documents {
        walk::document(doc, &mut visit);
        for view in &mut doc.views { for branch in &mut view.branches {
            if let Some(predicate) = &mut branch.predicate { walk::expression(predicate, &mut visit); }
            walk::shape(&mut branch.fields, &mut visit);
        } }
    }
    catalog.shapes = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| doc.shapes.iter().cloned().map(|shape| (shape.name.clone(), shape))).collect();
    catalog.functions = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| doc.functions.iter().cloned().map(|function| (function.name.clone(), function))).collect();
}
    fn collision_lookup(&mut self, table: &str, name: &str, index: &crate::schema::Index, key: &[Value], except: Option<&str>, effect: Effect) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let probes = super::rules::rule_probes(&self.schema.tables[table].rules.read);
        self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, probes)], |runner, allowance| {

                runner.observe_sequence_columns(table, row, &index.columns(), effect == Effect::Insert)?;

    }

    }

    fn ty(&self, e: &Expr) -> Ty {
        match e {
            Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer" => Ty::Scalar(Kind::Id(IdSort::Principal)),
            Expr::Name { name, .. } => self.vars.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown),
            Expr::Literal { value, .. } => match literal_scalar(value) {
                Ok((_, Some(k))) => Ty::Scalar(k),
                _ => Ty::Unknown,
            },
            Expr::Member { base, name, .. } => {
                let table = match base.as_ref() {
                    Expr::Focal { .. } => self.focal.clone(),
                    other => match self.ty(other) {
                        Ty::Row(t) | Ty::Scalar(Kind::Id(IdSort::Ref(t))) => Some(t),
                        _ => None,
                    },
                };
                table
                    .and_then(|t| self.module.tables.iter().find(|x| x.name == t).and_then(|x| x.col(name)))
                    .map(Ty::Scalar)
                    .unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown)
            }
            Expr::Read(read) if read.range.is_none() && read.filter.is_none() && !read.keys.is_empty() => Ty::Row(read.table.clone()),
            Expr::Call { name, .. } if name == "count" || CALENDAR.contains(&name.as_str()) => Ty::Scalar(Kind::Int),
            Expr::Exists { .. } => Ty::Scalar(Kind::Bool),
            Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } => if op == "not" { Ty::Scalar(Kind::Bool) } else { self.ty(value) },
            Expr::Binary { op, left, .. } => match op.as_str() {
                "+" | "-" | "*" | "/" | "%" | "??" => self.ty(left),
                _ => Ty::Scalar(Kind::Bool),
            },
            Expr::Assert { value, .. } => self.ty(value),
            Expr::If { then, .. } => self.ty(then),
            _ => Ty::Unknown,
        }
    }

    fn kind(&self, e: &Expr) -> Option<Kind> {
        match self.ty(e) {
            Ty::Scalar(k) => Some(k),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

fn resolve_key(table: &Table, keys: &[KeyPart], kinds: &[Option<Kind>]) -> R<Vec<String>> {
    if keys.iter().all(|k| k.column.is_some()) {
        let names: Vec<String> = keys.iter().map(|k| k.column.clone().unwrap()).collect();
        for n in &names {
            if n.contains('.') || table.col(n).is_none() {
                return no("key-column", format!("{}[{n}]", table.name));
            }
        }
        return Ok(names);
    }
    if keys.iter().any(|k| k.column.is_some()) {
        return no("key-mixed", format!("{}[..]", table.name));
    }
    if kinds.iter().any(Option::is_none) {
        return no("key-untyped", format!("{}[..]", table.name));
    }
    let kinds: Vec<Kind> = kinds.iter().map(|k| k.clone().unwrap()).collect();
    if let [Kind::Id(sort)] = kinds.as_slice() {
        if matches!(sort, IdSort::Plain) || matches!(sort, IdSort::Ref(t) if *t == table.name) {
            return Ok(vec!["id".into()]);
        }
    }
    let fits: Vec<&Vec<String>> = table
        .uniques
        .iter()
        .filter(|u| u.len() == kinds.len() && u.iter().zip(&kinds).all(|(c, k)| table.col(c).is_some_and(|ck| accepts(&ck, k))))
        .collect();
    match fits.as_slice() {
        [one] => Ok((*one).clone()),
        _ => no("key-unresolved", format!("{}[..] matches {} unique keys", table.name, fits.len())),
    }
}

/// The unique key selected by a rule probe. Named equalities may contain
/// residual predicates beyond the point key; the binder selects the shortest
/// covered total unique, then the first declared candidate. Positional probes
/// keep their declared-only, type-directed resolution above.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#14 — nullable residual membership equalities are distinct from the selected point key.
fn resolve_probe_point(table: &Table, keys: &[KeyPart], columns: &[String]) -> R<Vec<String>> {
    if keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_none()) {
        return Ok(columns.to_vec());
    }
    if columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id") {
        return Ok(vec!["id".into()]);
    }
    let total = |unique: &[String]| {
        unique.iter().all(|column| {
            column == "id"
                || table
                    .cols
                    .iter()
                    .find(|candidate| candidate.name == *column)
                    .is_some_and(|candidate| !candidate.nullable)
        })
    };
    let mut candidates: Vec<&Vec<String>> = table
        .uniques
        .iter()
        .filter(|unique| {
            unique.len() == columns.len() && unique.iter().all(|column| columns.contains(column))
                || total(unique) && unique.iter().all(|column| columns.contains(column))
        })
        .collect();
    if let Some(width) = candidates
        .iter()
        .filter(|unique| total(unique))
        .map(|unique| unique.len())
        .min()
    {
        candidates.retain(|unique| unique.len() == width && total(unique));
        if candidates.len() > 1 {
            let sets: BTreeSet<Vec<String>> = candidates
                .iter()
                .map(|unique| {
            WriteOp::Delete => {
                let key = self.write_key(info, keys, site, out).map_err(|error| self.extra_write_test(info, expr, error))?;
                if key.alternate {
                    let location = self.site();
                    self.sources.insert(location.clone(), (site.clone(), format!("delete {table}")));
                    out.push(Stmt::DeleteTarget { target: Read { site: location, table: table.clone(), index: key.index, key: key.key, provenance: KeyProvenance::Shape } });
                } else {
                    out.push(Stmt::Delete { table: table.clone(), key: key.key[0].clone() });
                }
                Ok(valued.then(|| (Expr::Literal(Value::Null), Ty::Null)))
            }
        }
    }

    /// `t[row]` where `row` is a present row of `t`: the same read as `t[row.id]`.
    fn row_as_key(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<ReadExpr> {
        if read.keys.len() != 1 || read.keys[0].column.is_some() || read.range.is_some() || read.filter.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.index.is_some() || !read.order_keys.is_empty() { return None; }
        let key = &read.keys[0].value;
        if !matches!(key, A::Name { .. }) { return None; }
        let (_, ty) = self.clone().expr(key, &mut setup::Body::default()).ok()?;
        if !matches!(ty, Ty::Row(ref table) | Ty::WriteRow(ref table) if *table == read.table) { return None; }
        let mut by_id = read.clone();
        by_id.keys[0].value = A::Member { base: Box::new(key.clone()), name: "id".into(), site: key.site().clone() };
        Some(by_id)
    }

    fn write_key(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, keys: &[ast::KeyPart], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<WriteKey> {
        if keys.is_empty() {
            return Err(unknown(site, format!("{} needs a key: which row?", info.decl.name), format!("{}[id]", info.decl.name)));
        }
        // A row value as the key addresses the row by its id.
        if keys.len() == 1 && keys[0].column.is_none() {
            let mut row_setup = setup::Body::default();
            let (e, t) = self.expr(&keys[0].value, &mut row_setup)?;
            if let Ty::Row(table) | Ty::WriteRow(table) = t.required()
                && table == info.decl.name
            {
                out.extend(row_setup);
                let id = self.temp("key");
                out.push(Stmt::Let { name: id.clone(), value: ir::field(e, "id") });
                return Ok(WriteKey { index: "byId".into(), key: vec![ir::var(&id)], alternate: false });
            }
        }

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/why/E_CASCADE_BOUND.md b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/why/E_CASCADE_BOUND.md
index a56c7ef04d..bbc777de02 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/why/E_CASCADE_BOUND.md
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/why/E_CASCADE_BOUND.md
@@ -1,3 +1,11 @@
 # E_CASCADE_BOUND
 
 E_CASCADE_BOUND means a delete's cascade has no fixed bound that fits its program. `on delete cascade` and `on delete set null` write every referencing row in the deleting transaction, so they need `cap N by <column>` on the referencing table: a product limit on how many rows one target can have. A plain `unique <column>` proves the same bound with a cap of one; a composite unique key does not. A delete then reserves the cascade's whole worst case — every cap along the chain — whether or not those rows exist, so its cost never depends on rows the caller cannot see; `check` refuses a delete whose reservation cannot fit a mutation (or a job chunk). For an unbounded relation: lower the cap if the domain has a smaller limit; keep `on delete restrict` and delete the referencing rows first in a job (`chunk`), then the target; mark the target (`archived: bool`, filtered by readers) and sweep it in a job; or declare `on delete keep` when the rows should outlive the target. guide writes.
+
+`check` computes a cap recipe for all children together that fits both rows
+and heap, including the root. Apply the caps together. A count-maintained
+source commonly reserves 3 × its row count: the source row and its old and new
+aggregate groups. Event images add heap as well. The recipe is a product cap,
+not permission to enlarge a meter. For a maintained sum source, keep restrict
+and bulk-delete ordinary rows in a system job so each deletion unwinds its
+contribution, then delete the marked parent after its children are gone.
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/why/E_UNIQUE.md b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/why/E_UNIQUE.md
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..244a3e7259
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/why/E_UNIQUE.md
@@ -0,0 +1,11 @@
+# E_UNIQUE
+
+E_UNIQUE means a caller-readable row already holds this declared secondary unique key.
+The refusal names `unique principal` (or the declared columns), never the
+planner's internal index name. Update the existing row or choose a new key.
+Declare `else CODE 'message'` on the key to use an application refusal instead.
+`insert … if absent` returns null on a readable collision when doing nothing
+is the intended operation. Every refused write rolls back the whole mutation.
+An intrinsic id clash remains E_CONSTRAINT; a hidden clash remains the ambiguous
+E_RULE (or NOT_FOUND for an unreadable write target). Keep private keys private.
+See `guide writes` for constraints that govern every writer across hidden rows.
//! Shared type-directed positional point selection for programs and rules.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — identity wins; otherwise ordered compatible declared keys only.
use super::catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo};
use crate::{ast::{IndexDecl, ReadExpr}, diag::Diagnostic, types::Ty};

pub(super) fn candidates<'a>(info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, types: &[(Ty, bool)], synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>) -> Vec<&'a IndexDecl> {
    let identity = read.index.is_none() && types.len() == 1 &&
        (types[0].0.required() == Ty::Id || types[0].0.required() == Ty::Ref(read.table.clone())
            || (!types[0].0.is_optional() && matches!(types[0].0.required(),Ty::Row(t)|Ty::WriteRow(t) if t==read.table)));
    info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
        .filter(|index| match &read.index {
            Some(name) => &index.name == name,
            None if identity => index.name == "byId",
            None => index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len(),
        })
        // Explicit indexes keep their existing mismatch diagnostics and range semantics.
        .filter(|index| read.index.is_some() || types.iter().zip(&index.columns).all(|((ty, literal), column)| {
            let expected = if index.computed.contains_key(column) { Ty::Int }
                else if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(read.table.clone()) }
                else { info.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown) };
            ty.comparable(&expected) || (!ty.is_optional() && matches!((ty.required(),expected.required()),(Ty::Row(a)|Ty::WriteRow(a),Ty::Ref(b)) if a==b)) || (*literal && expected.is_identity())
        })).collect()
}

pub(super) fn refusal(info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, candidates: &[&IndexDecl], catalog: &Catalog) -> Diagnostic {
    let alternatives = if candidates.is_empty() && read.index.is_some() {
        info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>()
    } else { candidates.to_vec() };
    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.7 — `budgets['general']` (lane 020): a text value names the declared
    // text key far more often than a row id, so that named form leads (and is --fix's choice).
    let mut alternatives = alternatives;
    alternatives.sort_by_key(|index| index.columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id"));
    let mut changes = Vec::new();
    for index in &alternatives {
        // Same-column opposite-order indexes need the explicit name as well.
        let duplicate = info.indexes.iter().any(|other| other.name != index.name && other.unique
            && other.columns.len() == index.columns.len() && other.columns.iter().all(|c| index.columns.contains(c)));
        if read.index.is_none() { changes.push(crate::repair::ReadChange::NamedKey { columns: index.columns.clone(), index: duplicate.then(|| index.name.clone()) }); }
        // Unknown explicit indexes and computed keys retain verified legacy alternatives.
        changes.push(crate::repair::ReadChange::Index(index.name.clone()));
    }
    let message = if alternatives.is_empty() {
        format!("{} has no declared unique key accepting these positional argument types in order; name the key columns, or use where for a population", read.table)
    } else {
        format!("{} positional argument types leave {} candidate keys; name the intended key columns or choose a compatible unique index explicitly", read.table, candidates.len())
    };
    let diagnostic = Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN", message, "").guide("reads").repair_read(&read.site, changes);
    // The user explicitly opts into one context-verified choice with --fix.
    if read.index.is_none() && candidates.len() > 1 { diagnostic.mechanical() } else { diagnostic }
}

//! Parent authority and lifetime checks shared by the storage adapters.
// @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — reserve before arm selection; resolve live, stamp, then authorize.
use super::{rules, runner::Runner};
use crate::{
    error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError},
    rows::{Bounds, Dir},
    schema::{Effect, Table, through::*},
    value::{Supplied, Value},
};

/// Minted only here: the stamped image re-enters the supplied-input channel
/// under reserve_capture's declared allowance.
pub(crate) struct CaptureInput(());

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) enum ParentVerdict {
    Readable,
    Unreadable,
    Absent,
    Unknown,
}

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn reserve_capture(&mut self, table: &Table) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let Some(through) = &table.through else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        if self.capture_prepaid {
            return Ok(());
        }
        let parent = &self.schema.tables[&through.parent];
        self.charge_price(crate::schema::reservations::capture_price(self.schema, table))?;
        self.capture_prepaid = true;
        self.read_tables.insert(through.parent.clone());
        self.rule_dependencies(&parent.rules.read);
        self.rule_dependencies(&table.rules.insert);
        Ok(())
    }

    pub(super) fn capture_insert(&mut self, name: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<Supplied, RuntimeError> {
        let result = self.capture_insert_inner(name, row);
        self.trace_authorization(&result, name, Effect::Insert, "read through capture");
        result
    }

    fn capture_insert_inner(
        &mut self,
        name: &str,
        row: Supplied,
    ) -> Result<Supplied, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        let Some(through) = &table.through else {
            return Ok(row);
        };
        let mut row = row.into_value();
        let id = row
            .field(&through.reference)
            .ok()
            .and_then(|v| v.as_str().ok())
            .ok_or_else(|| self.rule_refusal(name, Effect::Insert, "read through parent reference"))?;
        let parent = &self.schema.tables[&through.parent];
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — the parent lookup is authorization work under the
        // request's physical allowance; reserve_capture prepaid its logical charges.
        let found = self.authorized_scan(&[], |runner, scope| runner.tx.get(scope, &through.parent, id))?.filter(|r| !self.expired(parent, r));
        if self.device {
            let covered = if let Some(found) = &found {
                self.is_provisional(&through.parent, found)? || self.device_work(|runner| {
                    let viewer = runner.context.viewer.clone();
                    let now = runner.context.now.unwrap_or(0);
                    runner.authorized_scan(&[], |runner, scope| runner.tx.recording_covered(&through.parent, found, &viewer, now, scope, &mut runner.meter))
                })?
            } else { false };
            if !covered { return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("read through .{} needs a live, covered {} parent; queued without a row", through.reference, through.parent))); }
        }
        let found = found.ok_or_else(|| self.rule_refusal(name, Effect::Insert, "read through parent missing or expired"))?;
        stamp(parent, through, &found, &mut row)?;
        table.recompute_deadline(&mut row)?;
        if !self.context.is_system() {
            let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
            let readable = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &[], |runner, allowance| {
                rules::evaluate(&table.rules.read, &viewer, None, &row, false, &mut super::read::PrefixProbe { runner, allowance })
            })?;
            if !readable {
                // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#5 — only the developer trace names the unread parent.
                self.refusing_phase(&through.parent, &format!("insert into {name} needs unread parent {} through .{}", through.parent, through.reference), "read rule");
                return Err(RuntimeError::Rule { table: name.into(), effect: Effect::Insert });
            }
        }
        Ok(row.capture_input(&CaptureInput(())))
    }

    pub(super) fn physical_children(
        &mut self,
        parent: &str,
        id: &Value,
    ) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        let children: Vec<_> = self
            .schema
            .children(parent)
            .map(|(n, _, _)| n.clone())
            .collect();
        // Reserve the complete schema-fixed probe set before its first seek.
        // Never short-circuit on an occupied table, including at low ceilings.
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — the seeks draw from the request's physical allowance; occupancy never selects the logical charge.
        let heap = children.iter().map(|name| super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[name])).fold(0_u64, u64::saturating_add);
        self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::Examined, children.len() as u64), (MeterKind::Steps, children.len() as u64), (MeterKind::HeapBytes, heap)], |runner, scope| {
            let mut occupied = false;
            for name in &children {
                occupied |= !runner.tx.scan(scope, name, CHILDREN, &[id.clone()], &Bounds::default(), Dir::Asc, 1)?.is_empty();
            }
            Ok(occupied)
        })
    }

    // A deadline suffix skips an arbitrary expired backlog in one seek.
    pub(super) fn refuse_live_children(
        &mut self,
        parent: &str,
        id: &Value,
    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let children: Vec<_> = self
            .schema
            .children(parent)
            .map(|(n, _, _)| n.clone())

    /// A delete's own removal of one row, its rules already decided: receipt
    /// membership, the read-through child probe, constraints, maintains and
    /// the partition event. A referential cascade removes each row this way.
    pub(super) fn delete_row(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        self.receipt_member_removed(table_name, &old)?;
        self.refuse_live_children(table_name,old.field("id")?)?;
        self.check_constraints(table_name, Some(&old), &old, Effect::Delete)?;
        let entries = self.maintain_scratch(self.contribution_heap(table), |runner| {
            runner.tx.delete(table_name, table, &old)
        })?;
        self.charge_source_indexes(table, Some(&old), None, entries)?;
        self.row_changed(table_name, Some(&old), None, Origin::Source)
    }

    /// Remove a row the engine itself retires (an expired one): no rule and
    /// no constraint stands between the engine and its own sweep, while
    /// aggregates and partition events apply as for any delete.
    pub(super) fn remove(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Delete)?;
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        let entries = self.maintain_scratch(self.contribution_heap(table), |runner| {
            runner.tx.delete(table_name, table, old)
        })?;
        self.charge_source_indexes(table, Some(old), None, entries)?;
        self.row_changed(table_name, Some(old), None, Origin::Source)
    }

    /// At a program commit's end, retire every synced row the commit changed
    /// and every row it inserted whose lifetime has already ended: its final
    /// image was never live at a commit boundary, so it leaves no row for a
    /// later sweep to announce. Inside the commit the
    /// row existed as written (an insert-then-update still works). Engine
    /// bookkeeping, unmetered: the device's prediction does not do it, and
    /// the program's metering is already recorded. Its work, returned, stays
    /// linear in the rows retired: each aggregate row takes one net change
    /// and each audience key is scanned for a live row once. Seeds and loads
    /// keep their rows for the sweep.
    // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — a row whose lifetime ended by its own commit's end never exists at a commit boundary.
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub(crate) fn retire_expired_births(&mut self) -> Result<crate::meter::Metered, RuntimeError> {
        let Some(now) = self.context.now else { return Ok(Default::default());
        };
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.000.000#3 — an expired final image never grants a new recipient the row's id.
        // A row that existed before the commit and ends it already expired is
        // retired too: accounted from the partitions it was in at the commit's
        // start, it revokes there and announces nothing to a partition it only
        // entered with its expiring image.
        let mut changed: Vec<(String, String)> = self.born_rows.keys()
            .chain(self.touched_rows.keys())
            .filter(|(table, _)| self.schema.table(table).is_some_and(|t| t.is_expiring()))
            .cloned().collect::<std::collections::BTreeSet<_>>().into_iter().collect();
        if changed.is_empty() {
            return Ok(Default::default());
        }
        // The log's order must not depend on a map's iteration order.
        let order = self.schema.through_order();
        changed.sort_by_key(|(name,id)| {
            (std::cmp::Reverse(order.iter().position(|n| n == name)),id.clone(),
            )
        });
        let meter = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, crate::meter::Meter::new(crate::meter::Ceilings::unbounded(), crate::meter::Cut::Abort));
        self.retiring = Some(Default::default());
        let retired = changed.iter().try_for_each(|(table_name, id)| {
            let Some(row) = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id))? else { return Ok(());
            };
            let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
            if table.expired(&row, now) && !self.physical_children(table_name,row.field("id")?)? { self.remove(table_name, &row) } else { Ok(()) }
        });
        let aggregates = self.retiring.take().map(|retiring| retiring.aggregates).unwrap_or_default();
        let retired = retired.and_then(|()| self.apply_retired_aggregates(aggregates));
        let work = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, meter).used;
        retired.map(|()| work)
    }

   261	Every step is inside the mutation's transaction: a refusal anywhere (a
   262	`restrict`, a read-through child, the meter) aborts the whole mutation, and
   263	nothing commits.
   264	
   265	**Expiry is not a delete.** A parent whose `expire at` passes is absent from
   266	that moment, and the sweep removes it physically; neither fires an action.
   267	A child of an expiring parent outlives it unless the child declares the same
   268	lifetime (`read through`, or its own `expire at`). The writes card says so.

**NOT READY**

- **[P2] Expiry can invalidate the owner-equality proof under `restrict`.** [owner_equality.rs:33](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/owner_equality.rs:33) checks parent expiry only for kept references. With default or explicit `restrict`, a parent can expire while its child survives—expiry fires no referential action ([LLP 3000.039:265](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/llp/3000.039-a-reference-declares-what-deleting-its-row-does.rfc.md:265)). Ordinary insertion can then replace the expired parent with a different owner ([write.rs:200](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/write.rs:200)). The new owner’s parent lookup succeeds while the old child remains hidden, yet the proof suppresses `N_UNREADABLE_PROBE`. Apply the expiry guard independently of the delete action. Extend the expiry regression at [wave11_qdiag.rs:129](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/tests/wave11_qdiag.rs:129) with default and explicit `restrict` variants.
